# Deviations from DESIGN.md

Where the design was ambiguous or wrong, the build took the simplest option
consistent with it. One row per deviation; the id prefix names the work that
recorded it (`H` = B1 core harness, `R` = the rest of the B1 harness: milestone,
conform, drill, doctor, export, the CI recipe, `verify --e2e`; `C` = the B1
contracts).

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| H01 | The heavy harness logic is the Python package `sscourse` in the uv project `course/harness/` (stdlib only at run time; pytest and numpy only in its dev group). `practice/bin/ss` dispatches to `uv run --project course/harness python -m sscourse`. Each verb is one module under `sscourse/commands/`, so other work adds a verb by adding a file. | The design names `course/harness` as `sscourse` (5.1, 5.2); stdlib only keeps the root `uv.lock` and every learner env untouched. |
| H02 | `course/contracts/VERSION` (the source) holds only `semver`. Vendoring (`ss course init`, `ss contracts sync`) writes the learner copy with `semver`, `sha`, and `content_hash` (sha256 over every vendored file except VERSION). | A commit cannot name its own sha. |
| H03 | The Python contract pre-check is an AST comparison (every def and class in the unit's `.pyi` exists with the same parameter names), not `mypy.stubtest`. Rust and Go pre-checks are the manifest checks of 2.15; compile errors from the farm build report as "does not compile". | `stubtest` needs mypy and an importable package in the learner env; the AST check catches the drift that matters (names and parameters) with no dependency. |
| H04 | Rust uses one copy farm per learner, `.ss/rust-farm/` (and one per verify run), not `.ss/overlay/<ID>/rust/`. | Cargo fingerprints path packages by path and mtime. Per-module farms over one shared `CARGO_TARGET_DIR` reused another farm's artifacts: `ss verify course` saw a stubbed `acc.rs` pass using the reference build. One farm whose files are rewritten only when their bytes change is both warm and correct. |
| H05 | The Python `subst/` directory holds file copies (reference, stub, or mutant text), not symlinks. | Stubs and mutants have no file to link to; one mechanism for all three. |
| H06 | Units outside the dependency closure: C objects and the Rust and Go farms always use the stub of the current reference, even when the learner has a file; Python uses the learner's file when present and a stub (after the learner path) otherwise. | An unrelated half-written C, Rust, or Go file must not fail a compiled check; Python imports lazily, and 5.4 puts the learner's `python/` on the path. |
| H07 | The Hypothesis profile `ss` is registered by `course/tests/conftest.py` (always loaded through `--confcutdir`), not by `--with $ROOT/course/harness` as a pytest plugin. | Keeps the harness out of every learner env; same effect. |
| H08 | C stubs call `tl_set_last_error(msg)`, a setter the 2.4 `tinyllm/abi.h` does not list. The fixture `abi.h` declares it, plus `tl_alloc`/`tl_free` as the allocator seams units use. The rt.01 reference must keep the error slot and its setter outside the markers. | 5.2 requires every C stub to set `tl_last_error`, which needs a writable seam that still works when `abi.c` itself is a stub. The real `tinyllm/abi.h` (rt.01 work) should add these three declarations. |
| H09 | A C stub returning a non-status, non-pointer, non-void type is `return (T){0};`, not `return 0;`. | `return 0;` does not compile for struct returns such as `tl_arena_mark`; a compound literal works for scalars and structs. |
| H10 | Stub bodies are indented like the body they replace (C markers often sit at column 0). Go stubs rewrite imports the stub no longer uses as `_` imports. | Readable stubs; Go rejects unused imports. |
| H11 | A unit with no function bodies (a crate root that only declares `mod`s) is glue: it needs no markers, and check 5 does not ask for them. | 2.15 makes crate roots units owned by their first module; requiring markers in a file with nothing to stub would be noise. |
| H12 | `ss start` also stubs the sibling units of a Go package (not only a Rust crate root's submodules), so `go build ./...` in the learner repo compiles. | Same reason 2.15 gives for crate roots. |
| H13 | A dependency with no units (a primer, a practice module) never blocks `ss check`; it is noted. | `--ref-deps` has nothing to substitute for it, so blocking would be unrecoverable. |
| H14 | `ss diff` before a pass refuses unless `--spoil`, which records the module as spoiled. | 5.3 says "(after passing)" but not what happens before. |
| H15 | Practice-kind modules are checked by an executable `course/tests/<ID>/check` run in the learner repo; its exit code is the verdict. | 6.4 names artifact checks but not their mechanism. |
| H16 | A verdict line carries `assisted` (bool), `tainted`, `course_sha`, and `regressions` beside the 5.4 fields; `start`, `spoiled`, and `reset` are recorded as `event` lines. A later module counts as started once `ss start` recorded its start event; from then the earlier module's tree hash excludes the taken-over unit. | `ss status`, `ss export`, and the upgrade rule (5.2 point 4) need them. |
| H17 | Not built here: the mutation grade of `[learner_tests]` (B3), the solve checker and proof rubric (B2), `ss milestone`, `ss conform`, `ss drill`, `ss export`, `ss doctor` (other B1 work), `ss bench <ID>` (B8). The verbs are routed and exit 5 naming the batch (milestone, conform, drill, export, and doctor are built since: R rows). Verify check 6 proves committed mutants apply and are killed but skips the learner-test threshold; check 7 only parses keys. | Scope of the core harness task. |
| H18 | `allowed-deps.toml` pins are not merged into farm manifests yet. | No reference unit needs a third-party dependency before B4; untested code would rot. |
| H19 | Check 12 enforces the 8 MiB per-file and 50 MiB total budgets and manifest rows and hashes, not the per-batch budgets. | Fixtures carry no batch tag to sum by. |
| H20 | Check 14's AST lint covers Python (names imported from another unit must be in its `.pyi`) and C (units include only `tinyllm.h` and `tinyllm/*.h`). Rust and Go seams are enforced by compiling each reference unit against stubbed neighbours. | Parsing Rust and Go in the stdlib is not worth it when the compile already rejects an undeclared name. |
| H21 | `tests.dir` is honored for Python and C; Go and Rust course tests are found at `course/tests/go/<id_>/` and `course/tests/rust/<id_>.rs`. Go tests run from `course/tests/go` as `go test ./<id_>/...`. | A `module/path/...` pattern fails to resolve in workspace mode next to the dotless module `tinyllm`; the relative pattern works. |
| H22 | The farm `go.mod` drops the `replace` for the contracts module (the `go.work` provides it) and makes other relative replaces absolute. | A relative replace breaks once the module is copied into the farm. |
| H23 | `ss verify course` takes module ids and `--global` (global checks only); `--nightly` means three determinism runs; `--e2e` and `--kind` exit 5 until the milestone and export work lands (built since: R23). Global checks add "history snapshots match the owner chains" and "path stage order" (check 11). | Fast per-module runs for authors; the e2e and kind modes need the milestone runner. |
| H24 | `path.tsv` stage files resolve against the parent of the paths directory (`SS_PATHS_DIR`, default `paths/`, so the repo root as before). `ss next` treats a stage whose module checks all have a fresh pass as done even when it is not marked. Undo or a later real pass clears a forced mark. | Lets the self-tests run `ss learn` on a fixture tree; `ss next` should not send a learner back to a module they passed. |
| H25 | `_lib/pcg32.py` seeds as the reference `pcg32_srandom_r`, builds `uniform()` from `(a >> 5, b >> 6)`, and derives child streams with SplitMix64. Its tests pin the reference stream for seed 42, sequence 54. | `spec/pcg32.md` is not written yet; these are the standard choices and must match it when it is. |
| R01 | PyYAML is a harness runtime dependency (`course/harness/pyproject.toml`); everything else stays stdlib. | `ss conform` must read the YAML OpenAPI contracts; 3.2 lists harness deps anyway, and the harness env never touches the root `uv.lock` or a learner env. |
| R02 | Responses are validated by `sscourse/schema.py`, a JSON Schema subset (`$ref`, type, nullable, enum, const, required, properties, additionalProperties, items, bounds, pattern, allOf/anyOf/oneOf/not), not `openapi-core`. SSE chunks validate against the schema in an `x-tl-chunk` extension on the 200 `text/event-stream` content. | OpenAPI cannot describe an SSE chunk; the real `openai-subset.v0.yaml` (tracer contract work) must carry `x-tl-chunk`. The subset covers every keyword the contracts use without a heavy dependency. |
| R03 | Conformance cases (`course/conformance/openapi/cases/*.toml`) name a check implemented in `sscourse.conform.CHECKS` plus `params`, and carry `versions`, `tiers`, `smoke`, `requires`. Suites are named `openapi[:v0\|v1\|v2][:engine\|gateway][:smoke]` (the 5.10 example form). The request `model` is `--model`, else `[endpoints].model`, else `tracer`. | 5.8 names cases but not their format; behaviour needs code, the TOML keeps the list reviewable. |
| R04 | The gateway tier sends the key from the env var `[endpoints].api_key_env` names. The healthz case hits the health port when ss started the service, the base URL (or `--health-base`) otherwise. | 2.6 puts `/healthz` on the health port. |
| R05 | Conformance verdicts are ledger lines with id `conform:<suite>` (for example `conform:openapi:v0:engine`); the `conform:` path check resolves to that id. | One ledger for every verdict kind. |
| R06 | `system.toml` gains `[services.X].entry` (role, default the service name), `.section` (runtime.toml section, default the name), `.args`, and `[endpoints].model`. The generated runtime.toml forces the section's listen keys (gateway `listen`, `registry_listen`; engine `http_listen`, `grpc_listen`, `kv_listen`; durable `grpc_listen`) and `health_listen` for every section, written by the harness TOML writer (template comments are not kept). Services also get `TL_CONFIG`. | 2.16 says ports are filled into the config and `TL_*` overrides but not which keys; health is a separate port locally (2.6). |
| R07 | One process per `[services.*]` entry. Several engines on one host are several entries with `section = "engine"`. | Simplest reading of 2.16's "distinct ports" example. |
| R08 | Milestone specs gain `includes` (pass-gate composition), `services` (milestone or step), step `http = {method, url, json, headers, auth}` (the body becomes stdout; `expect.status`, default 200), the `sse` matcher (framing plus `min_chunks`), `ss milestone list`, and `--step`. Every command step must exit `expect.code` (default 0) before its matcher runs. | MS-P1's "curl with a key ... streams >= 32 tokens" needs a request step and a stream matcher; 5.7 does not say how a gate composes milestones. |
| R09 | `ss milestone` exits 3 when a `requires` module has no fresh learner pass. `--ref-deps` lets it run and records `assisted`, but substitutes nothing: milestones run the learner's own `[build]` and entry points. | D16: entry points are always the learner's; 5.3 lists only 0 and 1. |
| R10 | Without a reachable `[deploy]` cluster (kind missing, context or namespace absent), `ci = "kind"` steps are skipped with the reason and the verdict is `incomplete` (exit 1). `--smoke` verdicts carry `mode = "smoke"` and never count as the milestone. | kind is not installed here; a smoke pass must not satisfy a full gate. |
| R11 | `tokens-equal` near-tie rerun: by default the step's argv with `generate` replaced by `logits`, plus `--prefix-ids <expected ids before the divergence>`; the last stdout line is `{"logits": [...]}`. `near_tie.argv` overrides it with a `{prefix_ids}` placeholder. | `spec/cli-roles.md` is not written yet; it must define `logits` this way. |
| R12 | Not built here, exit 5 naming the batch: `calibrated` thresholds (B11), `file-produced` parquet `rows` (B6), the `perf` matcher (B8). `json-last-line` thresholds are `<op> <number>` with op in `>= <= > < ==`; the `ref*1.05` form is not supported. | 5.7 does not define `ref` outside calibration. |
| R13 | The `trace` matcher reads the Jaeger query API only (Tempo errors until Pass 7). A span tree means: one trace, queried by `service` over the run window minus `lookback_s` (60), holding every `spans` name, every `services` entry, and every `[child, parent]` pair in `parents`. | B1 names Jaeger; 5.7 does not define the tree. |
| R14 | `promql` passes when the instant result is non-empty and every sample meets the optional `bound`; `hold_s` re-evaluates every `interval_s` (15 s, `SS_PROMQL_INTERVAL`). | Comparison queries return empty when false. |
| R15 | `ci-status` treats `origin` as a GitHub remote when its URL contains `github.com`; the default branch is `origin/HEAD`, else the current branch. | 5.7 does not say how to find either. |
| R16 | `{asset:<id>/<path>}` resolves under `$TINYLLM_CACHE/assets/`; a `{date}` placeholder (UTC) serves drill postmortem paths. | 5.11 does not fix the cache layout. |
| R17 | Drill specs gain `symptom`, `step_s` (range step, 15), `[loadgen].argv`, `[evidence.trace]` (trace evidence is graded only when declared), and `at` = `start`, `+Ns`, or `loadgen+Ns`. Built injectors: `pod-kill` (`--grace-period=0 --force --wait=false`), `deploy-patch` (`env`, `args`, by JSON patch of container `container`), `scale-zero`, `resource-limit`, `config-drift` (`configmap`, `set`, then a rollout restart of `target`). The rest exit 5 naming their batch. | B1 builds the framework; ops.00 needs `deploy-patch`. |
| R18 | `ss drill end` grades from Prometheus `query_range` over [injection, end]: TTD is the first firing `ALERTS` sample; a `promql` check is resolved when true continuously for `hold_s` up to the end; TTM is the start of the last continuous true run. The postmortem is the newest file matching `path` with `{date}` as a wildcard, with every section as a heading. Over the time limit fails. A failed `start` undoes what it injected; `reset` re-runs the gate and refuses if `[deploy]` changed since the run. Gate refusals exit 1. | 5.10 lists what `end` reports, not how. |
| R19 | `ss drill status <id>` answers with the drill's last verdict, which the `drill:` path check uses. | The path bridge needs a non-interactive answer. |
| R20 | `ss doctor` requires git, uv, python >= 3.11, cc, cargo, go from Pass 0; docker (daemon running), kubectl, kind, helm, tilt from Pass 1; 6 CPUs and 12 GiB (0.5 GiB slack) from Pass 7. protoc, gh, and a Rust nightly are optional. The pass defaults to the highest pass among started modules. | 5.3 lists tools, not when each becomes required; the tracer deploys on kind in Pass 1. |
| R21 | `ss export`: a module that is not pass, assisted, spoiled, or self (todo included) is `incomplete`. The vendored files are committed (`chore(tests): vendor ...`); `--remote` sets `origin` and does not push. Go glue: `go.work` uses `../../go` and `./tests/go` and replaces the contracts module with `../../contracts/go` (a `use` would conflict with the learner's go.mod replace). The Python glue also puts `python/` and the tests dir on `sys.path` and defaults `TINYLLM_LIB` to `c/build/libtinyllm.*`; the printed Python command first builds that library (`make -C c`, else `cc` over the learner's C units). C test binaries go to the gitignored `c/build/`. | Vendored ctypes tests need the learner's own library; 5.13 is silent on push and commit. |
| R22 | `ss course ci [--upstream URL]` prints the learner CI recipe; no file is written. | P2: the course ships no CI file, but the recipe must be exact. |
| R23 | `ss verify course --e2e` runs only the end-to-end check, assembles the learner with in-process `ss start` calls, and commits the lock files the checks create before exporting. `--kind` assembles the same learner and runs every milestone with a kind step in full; with the reference cluster unreachable it fails. | Keeps the e2e within its CI budget; `ss export` refuses a dirty tree. |
| R24 | `ss check --all --ci` reports proof modules as `self` (skipped) and solve modules as skipped until B2; the cached full mutation grade arrives with B3. | B2 and B3 scope. |
| R25 | Verify check 12 also parses every milestone, drill, and conformance case, requires a MANIFEST row for each committed fixture a milestone step names, and resolves `requires` and `includes`. | 5.11: every fixture id used in a milestone resolves to one row. |
| R26 | The nine `openapi:v0` cases live in `course/conformance/openapi/cases/`, but the real `contracts/openapi/openai-subset.v0.yaml` is tracer contract work and is not written here; the self-test fixture holds a v0 spec. Until it lands, `ss conform openapi:v0` on the real tree exits 5 naming the missing contract. No CI workflow jobs were added. | Scope: this task builds the harness, not the tracer contracts or CI. |
| C01 | The umbrella `tinyllm.h` of contract v0 includes only `tinyllm/abi.h` and `tinyllm/matmul.h`. Every other header of 2.4 lands with its unit's batch and adds one `#include` line, as a minor bump of `contracts/VERSION`. | 2.4 lists the ABI v1 end state; a header whose unit no batch has built would declare symbols nothing implements. |
| C02 | `tinyllm/abi.h` adds `tl_set_last_error`, `tl_alloc`, `tl_free`, and `TL_LAST_ERROR_CAP` (256) to the 2.4 list, and fixes edge cases 2.4 leaves open: `tl_status_str` of an unknown code is `"TL_UNKNOWN"`, `tl_last_error` is `""` (never NULL) before any error, `tl_set_allocator` with a NULL function pointer is `TL_EINVAL`, and `tl_alloc(0, ...)` returns NULL. | H08: C stubs need a writable error seam, and units need allocator seams that do not reach into `abi.c`. |
| C03 | `tinyllm/matmul.h` forward-declares `typedef struct tl_pool tl_pool;` and documents `beta == 0` (C is written without being read), zero dims, NULL-pointer and leading-dimension errors, and that v0 may sum over k in any order. | `pool.h` (rt.03) is B8, but the matmul signature names `tl_pool`; C11 permits the identical typedef again in `pool.h`. |
| C04 | `contracts/c/ABI.md` is written in B1 although the B1 row does not list it. | 2.4 puts the ABI rules there and the headers cite it. |
| C05 | `openai-subset.v0.yaml` makes choices 2.6 leaves open: `/readyz` (gateway health port), 503 `no_capacity` when the gateway's upstream is unreachable, `usage` required on non-stream completions, `created` and `model` required on chunks, `prompt` non-empty, `max_tokens` 1 to 4096, v0 echoes `model` instead of routing on it, one chunk per token with `text: ""` while a UTF-8 sequence is incomplete, and unknown request fields ignored. | The conformance cases need a total contract; these are the OpenAI behaviours that cost the tracer least. Error-shaped `X-Request-Id`, `traceparent`, and SSE error events follow 2.6. |
| C06 | `formats/safetensors.md` states the canonical writer rules (dtype order, name order, compact JSON, `__metadata__` first, space padding to 8) from the `safetensors` serializer's behaviour; `course/oracle` does not exist yet to confirm them against the pinned version. The order of several metadata keys is not pinned, so golden tests use exactly one metadata key. The tracer tensor is named `bigram.weight`, F32 `[vocab_size, vocab_size]`. | 2.9 says the page pins the oracle's version, which arrives later; L0.0's golden test (bytes equal the library's) is the confirmation. 2.9 names no bigram tensor. |
| C07 | `spec/cli-roles.md`: the tracer engine takes `--health-port` besides `--model-dir --port`; the tracer gateway takes `--port --health-port --upstream` (its `--config` form arrives with gw.01, like the engine's with L10.5) and accepts the key in the variable `[endpoints].api_key_env` names. Pass 1 `tinyllm` verbs and outputs are fixed: `train bigram` (final line `{out, tokens, nll}`), `generate` (`{ids, text}`, generated ids only), `logits` (`{logits}`, with `--prefix-ids`, R11), `info --native` (`{abi_version, lib}`). | The runner always allocates and polls a health port (2.16); gw.00 has no config loader; matchers need exact output shapes. |
| C08 | `config/system.schema.json` is strict (`additionalProperties: false`; `[entry]` keys are the roles of cli-roles.md) and includes the R06 fields. The harness does not yet validate `system.toml` against it on every milestone (2.3); a self-test validates the 2.16 example and the fixture manifest. | Wiring the schema into `ss milestone` is harness work outside this contract task. |
| C09 | `formats/config.schema.json` allows a config with no `tl_arch` only when `model_type` is `llama` (read as `tl_arch = llama`), requires dimensions only for llama, and writes conditionals as `anyOf`/`not`. | HF configs such as SmolLM2's must load unchanged; the harness's stdlib validator subset (R02) has no `if`/`then`. Other architectures add their required keys with their modules. |
| C10 | `contracts/VERSION` stays `0.1.0`. R26 is superseded: the real v0 contract now exists, and `ss conform openapi:v0` against a running engine validates with it. | First contract content; nothing earlier to be compatible with. |
| L00-01 | L0.0's `tinyllm/io/safetensors.py` v0 also has `load_safetensors` (F32 only, the five reader rules), although 4.3 and `formats/safetensors.md` give the reader to L0.6. L0.6 still takes the unit over and adds every dtype. | The Pass 1 `generate` and `logits` verbs (`spec/cli-roles.md`) must load the checkpoint `train bigram` wrote; without a v0 reader that logic would live untested in the learner's CLI. |
| L00-02 | `BigramLM` adds `__init__(weight=None)`, the `weight` attribute (float32 `[V, V]`, saved as `bigram.weight`), and a `vocab_size` property to the four methods 4.3 lists. | The CLI saves the table and rebuilds a model from a loaded checkpoint; 4.3 names no way to do either. |
| L00-03 | L0.0 `used_by` is `["L10.0"]` only. L0.5 and L0.6 (its other call sites) are named in the chapter's beat 6 but join `used_by` with their batch. | The mirror rule (3.4) rejects ids that are not in the registry yet. |
| L00-04 | `lang.01` is in L0.0's `reading`, not `deps`, though the catalog Prereqs column lists it. | A primer has no code call site; `lang.01.toml` says the same. |
| L00-05 | `fit_counts` requires `alpha > 0` (ValueError at 0). The path row's "count-MLE NLL" is read as the NLL of the add-alpha count model (`alpha = 1` in MS-P1). | `alpha = 0` writes `-inf` logits and `0/0` rows, and `0 * -inf = nan` inside the one-hot matmul. |
| L00-06 | The L0.0 golden files come from a PEP 723 script, `course/oracle/L0.0/safetensors_golden.py` (`safetensors==0.8.0`, `numpy==2.2.6`), not from a `course/oracle` uv project, which does not exist yet. The bytes are identical under `safetensors` 0.4.5, 0.5.3, and 0.8.0. | Avoids creating the shared oracle project from one module. The goldens confirm C06 for single-dtype files: name order, `__metadata__` first and omitted when absent, raw UTF-8, space padding, no padding when aligned. |
| L00-07 | `sample` draws one `rng.random()` per token from `numpy.random.default_rng(seed)` and inverts the float64 CDF (first `j` with `F_j > u`). Tests at `temperature > 0` check repeatability and chi-square fit, never exact ids. | The tracer's RNG is not parity-checked until L0.5 moves to PCG32 (4.3); exact ids would grade the RNG call, not the model. |
| L00-08 | L0.0 commits 27 mutants although it has no `[learner_tests]` (rung R0). Each test's `CATCHES` was proven by running that test alone against each named mutant. | 5.12: every course test catches a committed mutant or is smoke or regression. |
| O0-01 | `ops.00` is a registry module of kind `drill` with no units and no course tests; `ss start ops.00` writes nothing and `ss drill end` is its verdict. Its `deps` is `["dep.00"]` (the deployment it injects into, also the drill's `requires`); the engine and gateway it triages (`L10.0`, `gw.00`) and `lang.07` are `reading`. | 6.4 grades drills by `ss drill start/end`. Listing build modules in `deps` would make the 3.4 mirror rule demand a drill in their `used_by`, which 3.4 reserves for call sites. |
| O0-02 | `craft.02` has no contract file: the ADR template lives in the chapter (section 4). The artifact check is `course/tests/craft.02/check` (H15), which runs the twelve annotated `test_*` functions in `adr_checks.py` (stdlib only; not named `test_*.py`, so pytest never collects it, while `ss tests` and verify check 10 read its annotations). The template fixes five `##` sections (Status, Context, Decision, Consequences, Alternatives considered), a status line `<Status> (YYYY-MM-DD)`, and `docs/adr/NNNN-<kebab-slug>.md`. | 3.2 lists `contracts/templates/ADR.md`, but templates are shared contracts outside this task and no B1 contract work wrote them; 4.6 asks for "ADR section lint" without fixing the sections. |
| O0-03 | The drill spec gains `[[doc]]` tables (`path`, `sections`) for documents other than the postmortem; `engine-crashloop` uses one for `docs/runbooks/engine-crashloop.md` (Symptoms, Diagnosis, Mitigation). The harness does not grade `[[doc]]` yet; until it does, MS-P1 checks the runbook file exists. | 4.5 makes the runbook part of ops.00's pass condition; 5.10's spec has only `[postmortem]`. |
| O0-04 | "Rollout healthy" is graded as `ss conform openapi:v0:gateway:smoke` against `[deploy].gateway_url` (completions flow through the NodePort, so a Ready engine is behind the gateway), not as `kubectl rollout status`. | Drill resolve checks are `promql` or `suite` only (R18), and there is no Prometheus in Pass 1. |
| O0-05 | The `deploy-patch` injection replaces the engine container's `args` with the tracer flags at the Kubernetes ports: `--model-dir /missing --port 8000 --health-port 9464`; the undo restores the learner's args. The target is `{deploy.services.engine}`, so `[deploy].services` names the unified engine under the key `engine`. | The tracer engine takes flags and reads no `TL_ENGINE__*` override (cli-roles.md, tracer form); 2.13 fixes 8000 and 9464 in k8s. 2.16's example lists only `decode` and `prefill`, which arrive in Pass 7. |
| O0-06 | Whether the drill causes user-visible 503s depends on the learner's readiness probe and rollout strategy (a probed `RollingUpdate` with one replica keeps the old pod serving and only stalls). The page says users "may" get 503, the chapter teaches the difference, and the drill grades restored service. | 4.5 expects `/readyz` failing; with default `RollingUpdate` (`maxUnavailable` rounds to 0) and a readiness probe it does not fail, and the built injectors cannot change the strategy. |
| O0-07 | MS-P1 `requires` lists all fourteen Pass 1 modules (`lang.03` to `lang.07`, `rt.01`, `M03.1`, `L0.0`, `L10.0`, `gw.00`, `dep.00`, `obs.00`, `ops.00`, `craft.02`); MS-P0's requires join through the spiral. | L10.0-4 | Tracer engine choices 2.6 and the v0 contract leave open: one request per connection with `Connection: close`; SSE bodies are close-delimited (no chunked encoding); request bodies need `Content-Length` (`Transfer-Encoding` is 501, none on POST is 411); head cap 16 KiB (431), body cap 1 MiB (413); `/healthz` on both ports; 404 and 405 (with `Allow`) in the error shape with codes `not_found` and `method_not_allowed`; a failure mid-stream is a `data: {"error": ...}` event and the stream ends without `[DONE]`. | The smallest server that passes `openapi:v0` and that `gw.00` and curl can drive; keep-alive and chunked arrive with hyper in L10.5. |
| L10.0-5 | Tracer sampling: a fresh PCG32 per request seeded `(seed, 54)`, f64 tempered softmax with one uniform and an inverse CDF, NaN logits never picked; without `seed` the engine draws one from std's `RandomState`. Integer JSON literals only for `max_tokens` and `seed`. | 4.3 says tracer sampling is seeded but not parity-checked; 54 is the frozen `_lib/pcg32.py` default stream; std has no other entropy source. |
| L10.0-6 | Span export: only `POST /v1/completions` requests produce a span, exported synchronously after the client connection is closed, `http://` endpoints only, 2 s timeouts; attributes `http.request.method`, `http.route`, `http.response.status_code`, `gen_ai.operation.name`, `gen_ai.request.model`, `gen_ai.usage.{input,output}_tokens`; status ERROR only for 5xx; `service.name` from `OTEL_SERVICE_NAME`, default `tl-engine`. | 2.11 names the span and its transport but not these details; exporting after the close keeps a slow collector off the request path. |
| L10.0-7 | `tl-sys/build.rs` (copied by `ss start`, not a unit) links `libtinyllm.a` statically (`links = "tinyllm"`) from `$TINYLLM_C_LIB_DIR` or `c/build/`, emits `rerun-if-env-changed` and `rerun-if-changed` on the archive already in v0, and only warns when no archive exists. | The harness's compile-only checks (verify 2, 14, the stubbed-tree lint) build Rust without a C library; a warning keeps crates that never call C building, and the rerun lines keep the shared farm from linking a stale archive. |
| L10.0-8 | L10.0 course tests write every model directory by hand into a temp dir (no committed fixtures) and read JSON with a test-local parser; there is no `tl-contracts::testing` yet (D35), and no float closeness is asserted. | Fixture budget 0; the code under test never checks its own output. |
| L10.0-9 | The reference engine entry point is `course/ref/entry/rust/crates/tl-serve/src/main.rs`; `course/ref/system.toml` is not written here (shared with the other Pass 1 roles). `exit_on_sigterm` uses `extern "C"` `signal` and `_exit`. | Entry points are reference-only (D16, 3.2); std has no signal API. |
| L10.0-10 | `course/mutants/L10.0/` holds 15 hand-written semantic mutants, one per chapter pitfall; no auto mutants. | `course/oracle/mutate.py` does not exist yet (B3); every committed mutant is killed by the test its `CATCHES` names, run alone. |

## B1 integration (`I`)

Rows from integrating the B1 parts. Each supersedes the earlier rows it names.

| id | what | why |

## Contracts for B2 to B13 (`K`)

Contract 0.2.0: every system-level contract of DESIGN 2.4 to 2.16 and 3.2, written before the batches that implement them.

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| K01 | Contract 0.2.0 writes every boundary contract of B2 to B13 at once and bumps `contracts/VERSION` to `0.2.0` (a minor bump: nothing in 0.1.0 changed meaning). It supersedes C01 (headers landing batch by batch) and C10. Not written: the per-module language contracts (`py/tinyllm/**/*.pyi`, `py/corpus/**/*.pyi`, `rust/tl-contracts` traits, the `contracts/go` interfaces and frozen `testing` helpers of D35); each lands with its module, whose registry `contract` field and pre-check pair it with the unit. | The task asked for the boundary contracts; module APIs are best written next to the reference that proves them, and every module batch already owns its `.pyi`. |
| K02 | `tinyllm.h` includes every ABI v1 header except `tinyllm/kv_pool_v2.h`, which ships beside it unincluded; `openai-subset.v2.yaml` ships too. The `kv/v2` and `api/v2` syncs of craft.13 and craft.14 become the wiring steps (the umbrella `#include`, `tl_kv_pool_create` accepting format 2, the gateway's version routing), not the first appearance of the v2 text. | D13 keeps v2 out of the running system until the migration; writing all contracts now required the text to exist. |
| K03 | C ABI additions beyond 2.4: `tl_pool_threads`; `tl_kv_set_fill`/`tl_kv_fill` (a block's filled positions, exported as `n_tokens`; `tl_kv_register` refuses a partial block); `tl_kv_pool_cfg`; `tl_kv_export_bytes`; `tl_crc32c`; `TL_KV_FORMAT_V1`, `TL_KV_MAGIC`, `TL_KV_ENVELOPE_HEADER`; `tl_fnv1a64` and `TL_FNV1A64_OFFSET` in `numerics.h` (M06.3); `tl_kv_block_ptr` takes a `const tl_kv_pool *`; `kv_pool_v2.h` declares `tl_kv_block_scales` and `tl_kv_formats_supported`. Struct layouts are tabulated in `c/ABI.md` and asserted by a test. | 2.4's pool cannot produce the envelope's per-block `n_tokens`; L9.4 reads through a const pool; units reach each other's CRC and FNV only through headers. |
| K04 | Edge semantics 2.4 leaves open: `tl_topk_f32` with `k > n` is `TL_EINVAL` (no clamping); an all `-inf` softmax row gives zeros; `tl_kv_register` on a hash another block holds is `TL_EBUSY`; a `tl_kv_lookup` miss does not set the error slot; a block hash of 0 becomes 1 (0 marks the tail); fp8 conversions saturate (e4m3 to 448, finite e5m2 to 57344, NaN to `0x7F`); v2 scales are per (layer, K or V, head) per block, where 2.9 says per (layer, head). | Kernels and pools need total behaviour for course tests; K and V of one head have different ranges. |
| K05 | `tl.kv.v1`: `PushKv` streams every block of the prompt, a deduplicated one with an empty payload, so the decode side learns the handle's whole block list in one stream; each payload is a one-block envelope and `KvChunk.crc32c` covers the payload. `InfoResponse.kv_formats_read` (7) and `DrainResponse.aborted` are added. | 2.7 sends only missing blocks but the decode worker must still reference the cached ones; mixed-version rollout (ops.04) needs the read list. |
| K06 | `tl.durable.v1` additions: events `WorkflowExecutionCancelRequested` (28), `TimerCanceled` (29), `ActivityTaskCancelRequested` (30), `ActivityTaskCanceled` (31); commands `CancelTimer`, `RequestCancelActivity`, `CancelWorkflowExecution`; `TaskTransition` and a `WalRecord` oneof so queue state (leases, retries, dead letters, redrives) lives in the WAL; `WorkflowTask.continue_as_new_suggested`; `ActivityTask.run_id`, `deadline_unix_ms`, `heartbeat_timeout_ms`; `Failure`; status enums. A retryable failure that exhausts its attempts dead-letters (the workflow waits for a redrive); `ActivityTaskFailed` is only for non-retryable failures. The WAL record CRC covers the 8 `seq` bytes and the payload. | 2.7 says the attribute messages are "fully defined in the contract file"; dur.08 cancel and compensation, dur.03 DLQ and redrive, and replay from the WAL alone need these. |
| K07 | `tl.raft.v1`: peers are numbered by `[durable].raft.id`; a follower answers client rpcs with `UNAVAILABLE` and the trailer `tl-raft-leader`; `Entry.data` is a complete framed WAL record; `AppendEntriesResponse` carries `match_index` and a `conflict_index` hint. | Figure 2 leaves client redirection and log-matching hints to the implementer. |
| K08 | Generated code: `course/oracle/contracts/gen-proto.sh` compiles the protos with `buf` (no `protoc`), runs protoc-gen-go v1.36.11 and protoc-gen-go-grpc v1.5.1 built into `$SS_CACHE`, and generates Rust with `tonic-prost-build` 0.14 from buf's descriptor set (`tl-proto-gen`). `contracts/go` requires grpc v1.83.1 and protobuf v1.36.11, whose `go 1.25.0` directive exposed a harness bug: the overlay's and export's `go.work` wrote `go 1.25`, which Go rejects; both now keep the patch level. `tl-proto` depends on prost, tonic, and tonic-prost 0.14. | No `protoc` on the machine and none may be installed; the module versions were already in the local module cache. |
| K09 | API v2 is selected per request with `X-TL-API-Version` (`1` or `2`) on the same `/v1/...` paths, echoed on the response; during the window v1 responses carry `Deprecation` and `Sunset`, and after it v1 answers 410 `api_version_sunset`. v2's new codes: `requests_limit_exceeded`, `tokens_limit_exceeded`, `queue_full`, `model_draining`. | 2.6 says v1 and v2 are served side by side but names no selection mechanism; OpenAI paths already contain `/v1`. |
| K10 | OpenAI v1 choices: `n > 1`, `echo: true`, and content-part arrays answer 422; `/v1/tokenize` is engine tier only; `Error.code` is an enum (adds `kv_handle_not_found`, `context_length_exceeded`, `internal_error`); `X-TL-Priority` is -100 to 100; admin keys are `tl_<12 base32>_<32 alnum>`; routes gain `backends` (canary weights) and `targets` in `{unified, disaggregated}`; `accept_if` has a fixed grammar. | Conformance cases need a closed surface; dur.12's canary needs weights in the route table. |
| K11 | Sampling and RNG: the per-request generator is `stream(seed, sample)`, with `child_seed = mix64(seed + id * 0x9E3779B97F4A7C15)` and `seq = id` for purposes init 1, dropout 2, shuffle 3, sample 4, mutation 5; normals are Box-Muller pairs with a spare; repetition penalty covers prompt and output, presence and frequency the output only; the reported logprob is taken after penalties, before temperature and filtering. The tracer (L0.0 numpy, L10.0 `pcg32(seed)`) predates this. The frozen `tests/_lib/pcg32.py` returns only the cosine half from `normal()` and derives `split()` differently: it is test data (D35), not the spec. | 2.10 names SplitMix64 "over (seed, purpose_id)" without a formula or ids; D11 needs an exact op order. |
| K12 | Formats added beyond 2.9's list: `bloom.md`, `wal.md`, `rag-index.md` (full `bm25.idx` layout), `checkpoint.md`, and the schemas `tokens-manifest`, `corpus-manifest`, `trainer-state`, `manifest`, `generation-config`, `export-spec`, `progress`, `tokenizer-json`, `tokenizer-char`, `eval-result` (row and summary). The chat template is read from `tokenizer_config.json` when `generation_config.json` has none (SmolLM2 keeps it there, checked on the local SmolLM2-135M-Instruct files); its Jinja subset includes subscripts. `trainer_state.json` stores PCG32 state as 16 hex digits. Token files keep 2.9's `[3] = vocab_size` and version 2 = uint32, which are course extensions (llm.c's Llama 3 files use magic 20240801, version 7). | Every artifact a later module writes and another reads needs a written layout; the SmolLM2 file must load unchanged. |
| K13 | The `tokenizer.json` subset accepts what real files use: `Sequence[Digits, ByteLevel]` (SmolLM2), `BertNormalizer`, `BertPreTokenizer`, the WordPiece decoder (BERT), `Metaspace` (Unigram); `truncation` and `padding` are ignored; `normalized: true` is allowed only without a normalizer. The schema accepts the local SmolLM2-135M-Instruct and all-MiniLM-L6-v2 (BERT WordPiece) files. The GPT-2 file was not available offline, so its `continuing_subword_prefix`/`end_of_word_suffix` allowance (`null` or `""`) is unconfirmed until B4's `tok-gpt2` fixture. | 2.9's "pre_tokenizer.type = ByteLevel" would reject SmolLM2 and BERT, which must load unchanged. |
| K14 | `config/runtime.schema.json` is strict (unknown keys are errors; `[ext]` is free-form) and adds keys 2.12 does not show: `health_listen` in every section, `[engine]` `model_id`, `advertise_host`, `queue_capacity`, `queue_deadline_ms`, `quant`; `[durable]` `raft_listen`, `long_poll_ms`, raft peers as `{id, address}`; `[worker]` concurrency limits; an `[agent]` section; `[gateway]` `upstream`, `key_cache_ttl_s`, `cache_ttl_s`, `affinity_load_factor`, `drain_deadline_s`. `config/system.schema.json` gains a per-service `env` map; the milestone runner does not apply it yet (B9 harness work). | The runner fills health ports for every service (cli-roles.md); disaggregated local runs need a per-instance `TL_ENGINE__ROLE`. |
| K15 | Observability: Go and Rust services expose metrics only by scrape on `:9464` (never also by OTLP), Python pushes its metrics by OTLP; dimensionless gauges use the empty unit so the Prometheus translation adds no suffix; instruments added for KV evictions, preemptions, KV transfer bytes and blocks, policy denials, registered workers, and WAL bytes; capped labels keep 64 values per process, then `_other`. | Scraping and pushing the same instruments would duplicate series; ops.11, gw.08, and L10.6 need their counters named. |
| K16 | Helm values schemas are JSON Schema draft-07 with `definitions` (what Helm validates; checked with Helm 4.3.0, which rejected a `latest` tag, a literal pepper, a literal secret-named env var, and a root container). `helm/observability.md` pins, from each official chart index, the newest release at least two weeks old: jaeger 4.14.0, opentelemetry-collector 0.173.1, kube-prometheus-stack 91.5.2, tempo 1.24.4, keda 2.21.0; rules carry `release: observability`. kind is not installed, so no chart was installed on a cluster. | Helm does not validate draft 2020-12 everywhere; "pinned" needs real versions. |
| K17 | `allowed-deps.toml` holds version requirements (majors, or minors for 0.x), not exact pins; exact versions live in the learner's lock files. | A requirement survives patch releases; drill ops.06 bumps a major on purpose. |
| K18 | `spec/cli-roles.md` gains an index of the verbs later milestones call, keyed by the first milestone; exact flags and output keys stay with each milestone file. | Fixes verb names now so batches cannot collide, without inventing outputs before their milestones exist. |
| K19 | `spec/subprocess-activity.md` fixes what 2.8 leaves open: the work directory `/artifacts/activities/<idempotency key>/`, the `DONE.json` completion marker (also the activity result), heartbeat details as the last checkpoint path, `Failure.type` `ExitCode<n>`, and SIGTERM to the process group with SIGKILL after 30 s. | Idempotent reruns need a marker; the runner needs a process group to stop Python's children. |
| K20 | Harness changes for the contracts: `ss verify course --global` builds `tl-proto` from a copy (so no `Cargo.lock` lands in the course tree) and runs `gen-proto.sh --check` when `buf` is on `PATH` or in `~/go/bin`; `test_contracts.py`'s v0-scope umbrella test now checks only that the v0 units remain; `tests/test_contracts_b2_b13.py` recomputes every worked example (KV envelope, Bloom bytes, token file, WAL record, PCG32 trace, sampling) with independent code. | A contract whose printed example is wrong teaches the wrong bytes. |

## Harness for B2 to B13 (`X`)

Rows from building the remaining harness work of section 9 (solve, mutation,
tdd, thresholds, parity, fetch, tl-py, sanitizers, bench, invariance, near
tie, zoo report schema, testkit, conformance v1 and v2, drills, scratch copy).
Each supersedes the earlier rows it names.

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| X01 | SymPy is a harness runtime dependency (`course/harness/pyproject.toml`), imported only by the solve worker subprocess (`sscourse.solve_worker`); the parent stays stdlib. One worker checks a whole set with a 5 s alarm per answer plus an outer timeout, not one subprocess per answer. Supersedes H17's solve part and R24. | 5.5 asks for a subprocess with a 5 s timeout; one process per set avoids paying the SymPy import per question. |
| X02 | Solve answers: lettered parts are sub-tables (`[q3.a]`, also accepted as `[q3a]`); implicit multiplication (`2x`) is allowed; intervals are written `[a, b) U (c, oo)` plus `{a}`, `{}`/`EmptySet`, `R`; matrices are row lists and a flat list is a column; a `basis` answer is a list of vectors (each inner list is one vector); `bool` accepts true/false/yes/no; `vars` kinds are real, positive, negative, nonnegative, integer, natural, complex. `ss start S-...` writes `solve/<ID>.toml` with one table per question and a stub file per proof. | 5.5 fixes the types but not their spelling. |
| X03 | Proofs: a question's `rubric` list, else `course/rubrics/<name>.md` (`rubric = "proof"` default); answers come from the terminal, or one `y`/`n` per stdin line; end of input is "not graded", never yes. A self-grade is recorded with the proof file's hash and reused while the file is unchanged (`ss check --regrade` asks again). In `--ci` an ungraded proof is `self (skipped)` and the set passes on its other answers; outside `--ci` it fails. | 5.3 says `--ci` reports proofs `self` skipped; rerunning a rubric on every check would make CI interactive. |
| X04 | `course/rubrics/{proof,design-review,postmortem}.md` are checklists (`- [ ] item`). `ss drill end` points at the postmortem rubric for self-review; it is not graded. | 3.2 lists the files; only the proof rubric has a grading rule (5.5). |
| X05 | Graded learner tests: `[learner_tests].path` is relative to the learner repo; `course/ref/learner-tests/<ID>/` holds the reference learner tests laid out from the learner repo root (so `python/tests/...` lands at that path). Optional class tables `[learner_tests.perf|model|agent|resilience]` take `path` or `cmd`; `model` adds `metric`, `higher_is_better`, `seeds` (5), `alpha` (0.05); `agent` reads `metric` and `ci = [lo, hi]` from the eval's last JSON line. | 3.4 gives one `path`; the R7 to R10 classes need their own suites. |
| X06 | Mutant kill rules: unit (auto, semantic) and resilience mutants are killed by a nonzero exit or a timeout; a mutated unit that does not compile is `invalid` and leaves the denominator; perf mutants must trip the perf suite; model mutants are killed when an exact one-sided permutation test over 5 + 5 seeds gives p < 0.05 (or the eval fails on every seed); agent mutants when the two 95% CIs do not overlap. Rust and C mutants and every model or agent mutant run serially; Python and Go in parallel (`-j`). | 5.12 names the classes and their bars, not the procedure. |
| X07 | `ss check` of a module with `[learner_tests]` uses the full grade cached by `ss mutate` for the current test files; otherwise `--ci` computes the full grade and a local check grades required mutants plus a seeded sample of 8 (`sampled` in the verdict). The grade runs only when the course tests pass. Verify check 6 caches course-test kills by (ref unit text, course test files plus `_lib`, patch) in `<scratch>/.course-verify/mutation-cache.json` and runs the reference learner tests through the same Grader. Supersedes H17's mutation part and the T3-11/L10.0-10 note that the runner did not exist. | 5.6 and 5.14. |
| X08 | `ss tdd red` and `green` run the graded tests against the learner's units with failing deps from the reference; red records per-file hashes, green needs a red with the same test hash, and `ss check` (rung >= 3) needs, for each current test file, a red record with its current hash before the last green of the current test set (ledger order, not timestamps). | Timestamps have one-second resolution. |
| X09 | The Hypothesis profile for learner tests is the plugin `_lib.ss_hypothesis` loaded with `-p`; course tests keep the conftest registration (H07). | Graded tests run with their own rootdir, where the course conftest is never loaded. |
| X10 | `ref-thresholds` live in `course/fixtures/ref-thresholds.tsv` (`key metric direction mode mean sd n threshold values`), written only by `ss milestone <MS> --record-thresholds [--seeds 5] [--smoke]` (json-last-line metrics `<= calibrated` or `>= calibrated`, key `<MS-ID>/<step>`) and `ss verify course <ID> --record-thresholds` (learning tests call `_lib.thresholds.check`, which records under `SS_RECORD_THRESHOLDS`); both refresh the file's MANIFEST row. The bar is mean + 3 sd (lower is better) or mean - 3 sd, sample sd over at least 5 seeds; smoke and full runs are calibrated separately. Supersedes R12's `calibrated` part. | 5.7 and 5.11 name the rule, not the storage or the tooling. |

Not built, and rejected at load with a message: `chaos.partition` (testkit `chaosproxy` link cuts), `leader:<glob>` targets, step `history`, `effects.linearizable`, and allocating every service's ports before the first starts. Supersedes B103-05 for the runner side. | B10-3 asked for these; only the parts MS-durable can run on one host are built. | X29 | The effects sink is served by the harness in-process (`sscourse/effects.py`, the wire protocol of `course/testkit/go/effects`: `POST /effects {key, value}`, `GET /effects`), not by running the Go package. One sink serves all the steps of a milestone's services, started before them when any step names it; `prefix` scopes the matcher to one workflow's keys (`<workflow id>/<activity id>`). | The Go sink is a library with no main; a Go build per milestone would make Go a requirement of every learner's MS-durable. The services take `TL_TEST_EFFECTS_URL` at start, so the sink must exist first. |
| X30 | A chaos kill is SIGKILL to the service's process group, then a restart with the same argv, env, ports, runtime.toml, and `{data}`, waiting for health; the delays are `uniform(every_s)` from `Random(seed * 1000003 + --seed)`. A restart that does not become healthy fails the step (not the milestone). Kills stop when the command exits, so fewer than `count` may land; the step detail says how many. | Restarting on the same ports and state is what a supervisor does and what recovery is graded on; a seeded schedule makes a failing run reproducible. |
| X31 | `{json:<step>.<key>}` reads a key from an earlier step's last stdout JSON line, and `json-last-line` thresholds expand placeholders, so a run under kills can compare with the uninterrupted run (MS-durable's pending `train-run-survives-kills`). Placeholder names cannot hold spaces, so the step name must not either. | The pending spec compared `final_loss` with an earlier step; `{out:...}` names only a directory. |
| X32 | MS-durable's `corpus-build-survives-kills` (6 kills of durable and worker, 0.2 to 1 s apart, seed 2) runs in the full milestone, not smoke: activities killed mid-run are redelivered after their heartbeat timeout, so it takes about 2 minutes on the reference (121 s measured). The pending spec's `every_s = "5..15"` was tightened because the uninterrupted build takes 1.3 s and no kill would land. Still pending: `kill-loop-500-appends-exactly-once` (the reference worker registers no `tl.test.Batch`; Echo's append lands as `<id>/1`, so a Batch keeping default activity ids needs `keys = "1..500"`) and `train-run-survives-kills` (fails on the reference without any kill: a second TrainRun of `specs/tiny.json` resumes the first run's step-000020 checkpoint in the shared `runs/tiny` and reports loss NaN). MS-durable-ha's kill loop stays pending (X28). | Reference behaviour outside the harness; the steps are kept commented with these reasons so they can be enabled when the worker and TrainRun change. |
| X33 | `ss drill start` runs `[workload]` (`role`, `argv`, `env`, `after_inject`, `wait_rollout`) through `[entry].<role>` in the background, logged to `.ss/drills/<run>/workload.log`, with a journal entry whose undo kills its process group; `wait_rollout` runs `kubectl rollout status <target> --timeout=300s` first and a failure aborts the start (undoing what was injected). `ss drill end` runs each `[[resolve.run]]` (`role`, `argv`, `env`, `timeout_s` default 600, `expect` default exit-code) from the learner repo and grades it with the milestone matchers as `resolve run <i>`; it counts toward resolved, not TTM. Supersedes B103-06. The chapters (systems/05-incident-response-and-chaos/02 and 03) and `paths/course-p08-durable/milestone.md` still say the harness does not run these. | B10-3 asked for both; chapter and path pages belong to their owners. |
| MSL0-01 | MS-L0's kill-and-resume steps read `course/fixtures/MS-L0/corpus.bin`, the MS-P1 corpus written as a 5,336-byte `formats/tokens-bin.md` file (version 1, vocab 256), not `small-corpora/tinyshakespeare.bin`. | `tinyshakespeare.txt` was never added (I42) and the harness does not derive `.bin` files at load. 2,156 tokens are enough for 100 steps of 8 x 32 random windows, and the milestone stays independent of a shared corpus owner (as O0-11 did for MS-P1). |
| MSL0-02 | The "kill" is the failpoint `train/after-step=60*crash` (exit 137, what a SIGKILL leaves), not a signal sent at a random time. The resumed process runs under `train/after-step=51*crash`, which kills any run that takes more than 50 steps. | Deterministic in PR CI. Without the second failpoint, a CLI that ignores `--resume` and retrains from step 0 prints the same final line and would pass. |
| MSL0-03 | Bitwise equality is checked with the `contains` matcher: the resumed run's stdout must contain the uninterrupted run's `<out>/final.json`. The line carries `loss_hex`, `data_cursor`, and `params_sha256` and no path or time. | No matcher compares two steps' outputs; `contains` with `file = "{out:<step>}/final.json"` makes the harness do the comparison on bytes the learner cannot tailor per run. |
| MSL0-04 | "Count-MLE NLL" in MS-L0 item 2 is the unsmoothed count MLE, whose NLL is the corpus's conditional entropy (2.076630290 nats per byte on the MS-P1 corpus; bar `nll <= 2.07763`). `train bigram` gains `--method autograd`; the default `counts` keeps the Pass 1 verb unchanged. | Gradient descent on the data's cross-entropy converges toward the unsmoothed MLE, not the add-one model of Pass 1; the Pass 1 form must stay valid (spec/cli-roles.md). |
| MSL0-05 | "Served by the unchanged tracer engine" is checked indirectly: the autograd model directory validates against `config.schema.json` and decodes the MS-P1 greedy ids, and MS-P2 reruns MS-P1's smoke steps after the learner's `[build]` step switches to `--method autograd` (the milestone page says so; nothing enforces the switch). | Services start before any step runs, so no step can point the engine at an earlier step's output. |
| MSL0-06 | `small-corpora/digits.npz` is the UCI Optical Recognition of Handwritten Digits data (dataset 80, CC BY 4.0) with its official writer-disjoint split, 3,823 train and 1,797 test rows, generated by `course/oracle/MS-L0/digits.py` from the pinned archive. | The design names the fixture but no source; UCI is the original, permissively licensed source (scikit-learn's `load_digits` is a 1,797-row subset of it). The reference reaches 0.965 to 0.969 test accuracy with standardized pixels, 0.953 with `x / 16`. |
| MSL0-07 | The gradcheck step asks for `checks >= 25`, the length of L0.2's op list with `gelu` counted once, plus `failed == 0` and `max_rel_err < 1e-5`. | The design asks for "every F op"; a count bar catches a suite that silently skips ops without fixing how `gradcheck_all` names its entries. |
| MSL0-08 | The reference CLI's Pass 2 verbs live in a second entry file, `course/ref/entry/python/tinyllm/cli_train.py`, called from `__main__.py`. They were written before L0.1 to L0.6 existed, against the 4.3 signatures plus the assumptions listed in that batch's report (BigramLogits holds one `[256, 256]` parameter; `save_checkpoint(dir=<ckpt root>, ...)` writes `step-<n>/` and `LATEST`; `load_checkpoint` returns `.step`, `.model`, `.opt`, `.rng_state`, `.extra`). | Keeps the Pass 1 entry readable and confines Pass 2 glue to one file the L0 owners can adjust. MS-L0 was proven against scratch stand-ins for L0.1 to L0.6 plus the real M06.3 and M10.3 references. |
| MSL0-09 | MS-L0 re-proven against the real L0.1 to L0.5 references (L0.6 `io/checkpoint.py` and `io/tokens.py` still scratch stand-ins). `cli_train.py` now flattens each `[B, T]` window to 1-D before `BigramLogits.forward`, because the L0.5 reference accepts only 1-D ids. A planted fault that saves `data_cursor.offset = 0` is not caught: in random-window mode every `next_batch` overwrites the offset from the PCG32 draw, so the offset is derived state and that fault is equivalent; the generator state is the cursor that matters, and dropping it is caught. | A bigram has no context across positions, so flattening is exact; the equivalent-fault note keeps the cursor claim honest. |
| M00-01 | `M00.2` has `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for it until `M07.0` (B3) or `L7.3`/`L5.4` (B7) lists it in `deps`. The other three have their call sites, registered concurrently: `M11.2` calls `bits_per_byte` (`M00.1`), `M02.2` calls `geometric` and `geometric_sum` (`M00.3`), and `M02.1` calls `horner` (`M00.4`). The catalog's Prereqs `M00.1` on `M00.2` to `M00.4` is conceptual (no code is called), so it is `reading`, as are `lang.01` and, for `M00.1`, `L0.0` (its worked example). | The 3.4 mirror rule accepts only registered modules in `used_by`. Inventing a call (for example `rotate_pairs` from another precalculus unit) would satisfy check 9 without a real call site. |
| M00-02 | `M00.5` (functions, inverses, monotonicity, inequalities; "solve only" in 4.1) has no registry file. Its content is taught in beat 2 of the `S-M00` chapter (`math/00-precalculus/00-solve-set.md`) and checked by S-M00 parts 1 (q1 to q10) and 6 (q53 to q60). | A solve-only module with no set of its own would be a second registry entry for the same 18 items; 4.2 already places them in S-M00. |
| M00-03 | M00.1 to M00.4 have no `[learner_tests]`: they are rung R0 (read the annotated suite), not R1 "fill the oracle" as 5.12 lists for math. Every semantic mutant has `required = n`. | The harness has no R1 mechanism (tests with `expected = ...  # TODO` holes checked against hidden values); `ss mutate` treats R0/R1 alike as "nothing of yours to grade". R1 can be added per module once the harness supports it. |
| M00-04 | The contracts for these units are 0-d tolerant: `log_base` and `horner` return a float64 numpy scalar for a scalar `x` (the contract says "a float64 scalar"), not necessarily a 0-d `ndarray`. | numpy arithmetic on 0-d arrays returns scalars; demanding a 0-d array would force `np.asarray` wrappers that add nothing for callers. |
| M00-05 | `quadratic_roots` scales a, b, c by a power of two before forming b^2 - 4ac, and `geometric_sum` returns +-inf on overflow; neither behaviour is in the 4.1 signature list. Both are in the contract and tested (`test_huge_b_does_not_overflow`, `test_sum_overflows_to_infinity`). | "No cancellation" in 4.1 is the point of the module; overflow of b^2 is the same class of floating-point failure and costs two lines. |
| M00-06 | Golden fixtures (40 KiB total, budget 1 MiB): `M00.1` and `M00.4` from mpmath 1.3.0 (60 and 80 digits), `M00.2` from torch 2.14.1 complex arithmetic (`view_as_complex` times `polar`), `M00.3` from transformers 5.19.0 (`LlamaRotaryEmbedding.compute_default_rope_parameters` and BLOOM `build_alibi_tensor`, both float32, compared at float32 tolerance). Generators are PEP 723 scripts under `course/oracle/M00.*/` run with `uv run --script`, not the `course/oracle` uv project (which does not exist yet). | Same pattern as `course/oracle/L0.0`; PyPI packages are the official sources (BSD-3, Apache-2.0, BSD). |
| M00-07 | The S-M00 key's independent recomputation, `course/oracle/solve/S-M00.py`, imports the shared `course/oracle/solve/_confirm.py` written by the concurrent S-M05/S-M07a work, and the chapter includes `problems.md` through the `<!-- ss:problems S-M00 -->` block that `course/oracle/solve/sync_chapters.py` maintains. | One mechanism for every solve set; S-M00 owns neither helper. |

## B3 solve sets S-M05, S-M06a, S-M07a, S-M08, S-M09a, S-M10a, S-M11a (`SV`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| SV01 | Solve chapters live at `math/NN-<slug>/90-problem-set.md` for an unsplit set and `90-problem-set-a.md` for part a (later parts take `91-...-b.md`, `92-...-c.md`, `93-...-d.md`), so they sort after the topic's build chapters in the generated `## Chapters` table. S-M01 and S-M02 use the same names; S-M00 kept `00-solve-set.md`. | 3.3 gives build chapter names only. |
| SV02 | Reference answers mirror the learner path: `course/ref/solve/<S-ID>.toml` plus `course/ref/solve/<S-ID>/qN.md` for every proof, written in forms that differ from the key's `expect` where the checker allows it (`binomial(10, 3)` for 120). No harness step reads them, so "the reference passes" was proved by hand for each set: `ss start <S-ID>` in a fresh learner, `ss check --ci` (exit 1, every question missing), copy the reference in, `yes y \| ss check` (exit 0) and `ss check --ci` (exit 0). | 5.5 and section 9 ask for a passing reference but give the solve kind no reference file or verify check for one. |
| SV03 | Each key is backed by a maintainer oracle, `course/oracle/solve/<S-ID>.py`, that recomputes every auto-checked answer by a route independent of the key (enumeration, brute force, numpy in the stated precision, SymPy differentiation of the actual function, exact rational iteration) and runs it through `sscourse.solve_worker.check_one` via the shared `_confirm.py`, which also fails on any auto-checked question left without an independent value. `course/oracle/solve/sync_chapters.py [--check]` keeps the chapter's `<!-- ss:problems <S-ID> -->` block byte-identical to `problems.md`. Both helpers are shared with the S-M00 to S-M04 work (M00-07). | Verification of a key must not rest on the author's hand arithmetic; 5.5 puts the problems in `problems.md` and in the chapter, which drift unless one is generated from the other. |
| SV04 | One item of the 4.2 table is one key entry (a question or a lettered part); every set matches its row exactly (52, 28, 30, 46, 20, 26, 18 items) and its rubric count is its number of `proof` questions (18, 5, 1, 4, 1, 2, 2). Each proof carries its own rubric lines instead of the generic `course/rubrics/proof.md`. Every auto-checked question has at least one reject canary naming the mistake it stands for (256 in total); for a solve set they play the part mutants play for a build module. | 4.2 counts items without defining one; a generic rubric cannot tell a proof of q20 from a proof of anything. |
| SV05 | Solve registry rows have `deps = []`, `used_by = []`, and `units = []`; prerequisite sets and the topic README sit in `reading`; `milestone = "MS-P2"`; `[tests].dir` points at `course/solve/<S-ID>`. Build modules name a set in their own `reading` (M06.3 reads S-M05 and S-M06a, per 4.1). | A solve set calls no code and is called by none (3.4 mirror rule), and the Pass 2 gate closes on every solve part of the pass (4.0 math gates). |
| SV06 | S-M08 asks matrix identities on concrete matrices, on vectors whose entries are named scalars (`[2*x1 + 2*x2, ...]`), as `choice`, or as proofs (q16, q20, q28, q34); there are no symbolic-matrix answers such as `X^T G`. | The checker's matrix type compares entries; it has no noncommutative matrix symbols. |
| SV07 | S-M09a q3(b) asks for the exponent $k$ of the smallest normal float32 $2^k$ (`-126`) instead of the value `2^-126`. | The exact-number check falls back to `nsimplify(got) == nsimplify(want)`, and `nsimplify` maps values below about $10^{-30}$ to 0, so `2^-127` and `2^-149` passed as `2^-126` (found by the reject canaries). |
| SV08 | Where a `choice` sits inside a lettered part, its options are words, not letters: S-M09a q8(b) answers `nan` (or `inf`, `0.5`, `0`) and q9(b) answers `one` or `two`. | Option letters (a) to (d) inside part (b) read as part labels. |
| SV09 | `ss check <S-ID>` passes only when every question passes (or is `self`), not at "80% or more of auto-checked items" (4.2). The sets were written for the stricter rule the harness implements. | Harness behavior, not changed here (see the shared-changes list of this batch). |
