## B3 group B3-4: M10.3, M10.4, M07.3, S-M01, S-M02, S-M03a, S-M04 (`B34`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B34-01 | M10.3 has `deps = []` and `reading = ["M10.2", "M02.2", "S-M10a"]`; the catalog lists M10.2 and M02.2 as prereqs. | `adamw.py` imports nothing from either: it follows M10.2's `Optimizer` protocol structurally and re-derives M02.2's bias correction per coordinate. Listing them in `deps` would make the 3.4 mirror rule fail M10.2 and M02.2, whose catalog call sites do not name M10.3. |
| B34-02 | M10.4 has `deps = []` and `reading = ["M00.3", "M10.2"]`. | The schedules are closed-form functions of `step` and clipping touches only `grad`; nothing of M00.3 or M10.2 is called (same mirror-rule reason as B34-01). |
| B34-03 | `wsd` warms up linearly from 0, like `cosine_with_warmup`; HF's `get_wsd_schedule` with `min_lr_ratio > 0` starts its warmup at `min_lr_ratio * lr_max`. The golden case `wsd_linear_floor` is compared from step `warmup` on (`compare_from`); `wsd_linear_to_zero` is compared at every step. | One warmup rule for every schedule in the contract (`lr_max * step / warmup`), so the train loop and C1 configs need no per-schedule warmup knowledge; the stable and decay phases, which decide the result, match HF exactly. |
| B34-04 | M07.3 has `deps = ["M07.0"]` and `reading = ["M01.3", "M06.3", "S-M02", "S-M04"]`; the catalog lists M01.3 as a prereq. | The gains are constants (`calculate_gain`), so no M01.3 code is called; the generator is passed in by the caller (course tests pass the frozen PCG32, D35). Only `tinyllm.prob.rv.normal` is imported. |
| B34-05 | The three build modules list only the Pass 2 call sites: `used_by = ["L0.4"]` for M07.3, `["L0.5", "L0.6"]` for M10.3 (L0.6 checkpoints its state), `["L0.5"]` for M10.4. Their other catalog call sites (L3.2, L3.6, L4.1, L5.5, L6.1, L7.9, C1, L12.1) join with their batches. | The mirror rule only accepts call sites that exist in the registry, and a build module needs one (P1). L0.4 to L0.6 are authored by another B3 group at the same time; their registry rows name these modules in `deps`. |
| B34-06 | M10.3 keeps one global step counter `t` (one `step` in `formats/checkpoint.md`); torch keeps `state["step"]` per parameter. A parameter whose grad is `None` is skipped (no decay, no moment update) while `t` still advances. | The two agree whenever every parameter has a gradient on every step, which is how the course trains and how the torch goldens were recorded; one counter is what the checkpoint format stores. The chapter (section 4) and "Going further" state the difference. |
| B34-07 | `clip_grad_norm_` returns a non-finite total norm and leaves the gradients unchanged; torch (with `error_if_nonfinite=False`) multiplies by `max_norm / (inf + 1e-6) = 0`, zeroing them (or by NaN). When the coefficient is at least 1 the gradients are not touched, where torch multiplies by the clamped 1.0 (bitwise the same result). | Zeroed gradients hide an overflow and still advance the step; returning `inf` lets the train loop (L0.5) and mixed precision (L11.1) skip the step, as AMP's `GradScaler` does. All finite torch goldens match exactly. |
| B34-08 | The optimizer fixtures are three per-module JSON files, not one `optim-torch` `.npz`: `course/fixtures/M10.3/adam_torch.json` (71 518 bytes), `M10.4/schedule_hf.json` (10 890), `M07.3/init_torch.json` (3 326), 85 734 bytes in total. Generators are PEP 723 scripts in `course/oracle/<ID>/` pinned to `torch==2.14.1`, `transformers==5.19.0`, `numpy==2.4.6`, run offline from the uv cache; a rerun reproduces all three byte for byte. | Each module owns its fixture (5.2 ownership by file), JSON float reprs round-trip float64 exactly, and the files are small enough to review in a diff. Torch generates nothing at test time (the tests use only numpy and the frozen helpers). |
| B34-09 | M10.3, M10.4, and M07.3 have no `[learner_tests]` (rung R0) and use `milestone = "MS-P2"`; the four solve sets also use `MS-P2`. | Same reasons as B41-08 and B41-09: rung R1 holes are not implemented by the harness, and 4.0 closes a pass with `ss check` on every math module and solve part it teaches. |
| B34-10 | M07.3's interface adds to the catalog row: `fans`, `calculate_gain` (torch's table), `xavier_uniform`, `xavier_normal`, `kaiming_normal` (no `kaiming_uniform`), `normal_init`, and `scaled_residual_std` (GPT-2's `base / sqrt(2 * n_layers)`). Every initializer returns float32 filled in C order from the caller's PCG32 (`rng.uniform()` for uniforms, `tinyllm.prob.rv.normal` for normals), not from torch's generator; the torch fixture pins only fans and gains, and the distributions are checked statistically and against the spec draws. | Callers (L0.4 layers, L3.2 recurrent weights, C1) need exactly these; drawing from the spec PCG32 is what makes a seed fix the weights in every language (P11, D10). |

## B3 group B3-3: M09.2, M11.1, M08.1, M08.2, M08.3, M10.1, M10.2 (`B33`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B33-01 | `used_by` lists the call sites registered today (the L0 rows landed during this work): M09.2 `[M11.1, M08.3, L0.2, L0.3]`, M11.1 `[M08.3, L0.3]`, M08.1 `[M08.2, L0.2]`, M08.2 `[L0.1]`, M08.3 `[L0.1, L0.2, L0.3]`, M10.1 `[M10.2]`, M10.2 `[L0.5]`. M08.3 gains L0.1, which the catalog row omits: the Tensor backward calls `unbroadcast`. The other catalog call sites (L8.1, L6.7, L9.2 for M09.2; L8.1, L8.6, L12.3 for M11.1; L3.1, L7.1 for M08.3; L2.2, L3.6 for M10.2) join with their batches. | The 3.4 mirror rule accepts exactly the registered modules whose `deps` contain the module; a later batch adds its id to these rows when its registry row lands (listed in this group's shared changes). |
| B33-02 | Catalog prerequisites that are concepts, not calls, sit in `reading`: M09.2 reads M09.1, M02.1; M08.1 reads M02.1; M08.3 reads M03.1; M10.1 reads M04.1, M03.4. `deps` holds only modules whose code a unit or course test calls (M08.1: M01.3, M04.2; M08.2: M06.1, M04.2, M08.1; M08.3: M09.2, M11.1, M04.2; M11.1: M09.2; M10.2: M10.1). | Same rule as B41-03: listing a concept as a dep would make the 3.4 mirror rule demand a call site in the prerequisite. |
| B33-03 | M10.1's catalog Tests column lists O (golden); its course tests use an exact oracle instead: `test_exact_rational_trajectory` recomputes 12 steps of gradient descent with `fractions.Fraction` inside the test, and the Armijo tests check the condition and the largest acceptable grid step directly. No fixture is committed for M10.1. | No oracle library implements the contract's backtracking search (scipy's `line_search_armijo` interpolates with quadratics and cubics), and plain gradient descent in torch is `SGD` with momentum 0, which M10.2's torch golden already records. An exact rational recomputation is a stronger oracle than a recorded float trajectory. |
| B33-04 | M08.1's differential test compares Dual derivatives with M01.3's closed forms at rtol 1e-12, atol 1e-14, not "to 1e-15". | M01.3's `erf` is a 160-term series (M02.1) within about 3e-15 of the true erf, so `1 + erf(x)` near x = -6 differs by a few 1e-15 between two correct float64 evaluations; 1e-15 would fail correct code. |
| B33-05 | Interfaces add to the catalog: M09.2 fixes the fully masked row (all `-inf`: softmax zeros, log-sum-exp and log-softmax `-inf`, no warning) and keeps float dtypes; M11.1 adds `kl_from_logprobs` (KL from log-probabilities, the form model outputs need) and `entropy_from_logits`; M08.1 adds `dual_erf`, `derivative`, `jvp`, vector duals with `@` and indexing; M08.3's `cross_entropy_vjp` takes `ignore_index = -100` and returns the gradient of the mean over non-ignored targets, as torch's `F.cross_entropy`; M10.2's `SGD` exposes `lr` as a writable attribute that schedules set before each step. | Each is needed by a named call site (L0.2 padding masks, L0.3 ignore_index, L12.3 log-probabilities, M10.4 schedules) or by the golden comparison. |
| B33-06 | The seven modules have no `[learner_tests]` (rung R0) and use `milestone = "MS-P2"`. | Same reasons as B41-08 and B41-09: rung R1 holes are not implemented by the harness, and MS-P2 is the pass gate that lists every math module of the pass. |
| B33-07 | Fixtures (78 KiB of the B3 6 MiB budget): M09.2 and M11.1 from scipy 1.17.1, M08.3 and M10.2 from torch 2.14.1, each by a PEP 723 script under `course/oracle/<ID>/` (`uv run --script`, packages from PyPI, BSD-3 and BSD-style licenses; fixtures Apache-2.0). Rerun in a scratch directory on 2026-10-09, all four regenerate byte for byte (same sha256 as MANIFEST.tsv). | The 5.11 fixture rule, with the regeneration as evidence that the generators are the source of truth. |
| B33-08 | M10.1 and M10.2 gain one test each beyond the first draft: `test_armijo_keeps_halving` (a search that needs two halvings; with the 2^-60 boundary in `test_armijo_gives_up`) and `test_lr_change_takes_effect_on_the_next_step` (the momentum buffer holds gradients, so a changed `lr` scales the next whole step). Both chapters gained the matching pitfall row (M10.1 item 10, M10.2 item 9) and mutants (M10.1 `s12`, `m03`, `m04`; M10.2 `s11`, `m03`, `m04`), bringing every module of the group to 15 to 20 committed mutants. | 5.6 targets 15 to 40 mutants per module; the M10.2 mutant `s11` (learning rate folded into the buffer) matches torch's trajectory exactly while `lr` is constant, so only a changed `lr` exposes it, and M10.4's schedules change it every step. |

## B3 group 2: M06.1, M06.3, M03.2, M03.3, M03.4, M07.0, M09.1

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| M061-01 | `used_by` names only registered call sites, mirrored from their `deps`: M06.1 `["M08.2", "L0.1"]`, M06.3 `["M07.0", "L0.2", "L0.4", "L0.5", "L0.6"]`, M09.1 `["L0.6"]`, M03.2 and M03.3 `["M03.4"]`, M07.0 `["M03.3", "M07.3"]`. The other 4.1 call sites (L8.7; L8.1, L6.2, data.04, rt.04; L7.9, L11.1, M09.4; M07.7, M10.5; L0.4 init, L3.1, L3.2, M03.5) join when their batches register them, and each chapter's beat 6 already lists them. M06.1's prerequisite M05.4 is not in the registry, so its track `math/05-discrete-math-1` stands in as `reading`. | The 3.4 mirror rule rejects unregistered ids in `used_by` and requires every registered caller. The L0 rows of B3 landed while this group was being verified, so the three rows were updated to mirror them. |
| M061-02 | Prerequisites that are concepts, not calls, sit in `reading`: M03.2 reads M03.1, M03.3 reads M03.2, M07.0 reads M00.2 and `math/02-calculus-2` (S-M02 stands for the Gaussian integral). Calls the catalog does not list are `deps`: M03.3 depends on M07.0 (`orthogonal_init` draws its Gaussian matrix with `tinyllm.prob.rv.normal`), and M03.4 depends on M03.2 as well as M03.3 (`inverse_iteration` factors once with `lu` and solves with `lu_solve`; `spectral_radius` iterates `qr_householder`). | `deps` drives the overlay and `--ref-deps`; a module that imports another must list it, and one that only builds on its ideas must not. |
| M063-01 | Interfaces add to the 4.1 signatures, all from `spec/pcg32.md` or needed by a test of the chapter's math: `PCG32.below`, `PCG32.shuffle`, `PCG32.seed`, `child_seed`, and the constants `MULT`, `GOLDEN`, `FNV_OFFSET`, `FNV_PRIME`, `PURPOSES` (M06.3); `inverse_iteration` (M03.4); `box_muller` (M07.0); `compose_f32` (M09.1). `orthogonal_init` and `power_iteration` type `rng` as `Any`: anything with `uniform()`, so course tests pass the frozen `tests/_lib/pcg32.py` (D35) and learners pass their own. | `below` and `shuffle` are spec operations (the frozen `tests/_lib/pcg32.py` has `below` too, and data loaders shuffle with them); the other additions are the smallest pieces the worked examples and pitfalls need to be tested on their own. |
| M063-02 | M06.3's registry `contract` lists only `contracts/py/tinyllm/num/rng.pyi`. Its C half implements the M06.3 section of the shared `tinyllm/numerics.h` (`tl_pcg32_seed`, `tl_pcg32_next`, `tl_pcg32_uniform`, `tl_fnv1a64`), which the chapter card links, but that header carries no `chapter:` back-pointer. | One header serves M06.3 and M09.4 to M09.6; the 6.5 back-pointer rule allows one chapter per contract file, and the header belongs to the contracts batch. |
| M063-03 | `course/fixtures/M06.3/pcg32.vectors.json` is a byte copy of `contracts/spec/pcg32.vectors.json`. `course/oracle/M06.3/vectors.py` rebuilds it with the independent stdlib generator and refuses to write unless it equals the contract file (`--check` compares only). M06.3 and M07.0 tests read the fixture. | A course test cannot reach `contracts/` once `ss export` vendors the tests: only fixtures travel with them. |
| M070-01 | `normal(rng, n)` keeps no spare between calls: each pair draws `u1`, `u2` and gives the cosine then the sine, and an odd `n` drops the last sine. `spec/pcg32.md` describes a generator-level `normal()` whose spare carries across calls. Both agree for even `n` from a fresh generator, which is what the vectors check: the tests compare 8 normals for seeds 0, 1, and 2^63 within 4 ulp (the spec's tolerance), not "the first 16 normals for seed 0" of 4.1 (the vectors file holds 8 per seed). | A pure function of (generator state, n) needs no hidden state, so `PCG32.state()` stays `(state, inc)` and resuming a run cannot lose a spare. The Rust and Go ports that call `normal` in arrays should follow one rule; see the shared change in this group's report. |
| M034-02 | M03.4 has `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for it. Its 4.1 call sites are L0.5 (training monitor), L3.1 (exploding-gradient diagnostic), and M10.5; L0.5 is registered but does not list M03.4 in `deps`, and M10.1 lists it only in `reading`. | Inventing a call to satisfy check 9 is worse than a visible failure; the fix is L0.5 calling `spectral_radius` in its monitor, or the call site landing with B6. |
| M091-01 | M09.1's golden file `course/fixtures/M09.1/round_f32.npz` (10 156 float32 bit patterns) is rounded by exact rational arithmetic (`fractions.Fraction`) in `course/oracle/M09.1/lowp_golden.py`, with numpy's float32 to float16 cast as a second, independent check of the f16 column, not by torch's bf16 conversion as 4.1 says. Regenerating gives identical arrays (the npz hash differs only by zip timestamps). | torch is not installed and nothing may be installed; exact rounding is a stronger oracle than another float implementation. |
| M091-02 | The mutant patches of this group are diffs against the marker-free reference (what `verify` check 6 patches), with the manifest `line` column set to the first changed line of that text. | Patches written against the marked reference failed to apply (marker lines in their context) or applied only by offset. |

## B4 group 2: L1.1, L1.2, L1.3, L1.4, L1.6, S-M07b (`L1`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| L1-01 | Fixtures (4.0 MiB of the B4 6 MiB budget): `tok-gpt2`, `tok-smollm2`, and `tok-bert` commit each model's official `tokenizer.json` unchanged (sha256 equal to the pins in `course/oracle/tok/golden.py`: openai-community/gpt2@607a30d, MIT; HuggingFaceTB/SmolLM2-135M@93efa2f and google-bert/bert-base-uncased@86b5e09, Apache-2.0) plus `cases.jsonl` for 300 shared strings computed by `tokenizers==0.22.1`, GPT-2's cross-checked by `tiktoken==0.12.0`; the L1.4 Unigram pair comes from the Hugging Face UnigramTrainer and from `sentencepiece==0.2.0` (236 strings on which both libraries agree); `L1.6/metrics.json` from the same library. GPT-2's `vocab.json` and `merges.txt` and BERT's `vocab.txt` are not committed: the oracle asserts they equal what `tokenizer.json` holds, and the tests derive them. `L1.2/train.txt` is a hand-written 3 KB story. | The design names these three fixture sets for B4; deriving the classic files keeps the budget. |
| L1-02 | `used_by` lists only registered call sites: L1.1 `[L1.2, L1.3, L1.4, L1.6]`, L1.2 `[L1.5, L1.6]`, L1.3 `[L1.6]`, L1.4 `[L1.6]`. L1.6 has `deps = [L1.1, L1.2, L1.3, L1.4, M11.2]` because its course tests load GPT-2, SmolLM2, BERT, and a Unigram through the L1.2 to L1.4 loaders and turn bytes per token into bits per byte with M11.2's `NLLAccumulator`. The catalog's later call sites (L3.6 for L1.1; L7.9, L8.2, C1 for L1.2; L6.2, L6.3 for L1.3; C1 for L1.4) join with their batches. | The 3.4 mirror rule accepts only registered modules whose `deps` name the module. L1.6 measuring the real tokenizers is the mechanism of the C1 ablation, so it is a true call site, not one made up for check 9. |
| L1-03 | L1.6 has `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for it until `data.07` (B5) or `C1` (B11) lists L1.6 in `deps`. | Same situation as B41-04 and M034-02: no registered module calls the metrics yet. |
| L1-04 | The loaders accept, per family, only what real files of that family use, and raise `ValueError` naming the field otherwise: BPE needs `normalizer` null and a `post_processor` that is null or ByteLevel; Unigram needs `normalizer` null and Metaspace with `prepend_scheme` "always"; WordPiece needs `BertNormalizer` with `clean_text` and `handle_chinese_chars` true. `formats/tokenizer.md` lists NFC, NFKC, Lowercase, and Sequence normalizers for the whole subset. | GPT-2, SmolLM2, BERT, and the trainers' outputs need nothing more, and a normalizer path no fixture exercises would be untested code that changes ids. |
| L1-05 | L1.2's catalog `B` (1 MB train budget) is a Python `[bench]`: `course/tests/L1.2/bench/train_1mb.py` trains 4096 tokens on 1 MiB of deterministic synthetic text, with the absolute budget `mb_per_s >= 0.1`, not a multiple of a calibration metric. Reference: 3.17 MB/s. | The calibration metrics are a C matmul and a decode step; a Python trainer's speed is set by the algorithm (incremental heap updates against recounting every pair per merge, about 0.005 MB/s), which a fixed bound separates by 30x on either side. |
| L1-06 | Unigram Viterbi ties go to the path whose last piece starts earliest (sentencepiece's lattice and Hugging Face's `encode_optimized` keep the first candidate at the best score, scanning start positions in order); `contracts/py/tinyllm/tok/unigram.pyi` and the tests say so. `formats/tokenizer.md` says "ties go to the segmentation whose first differing piece is longer", which picks `▁ aa a` where both libraries give `▁ a aa`. | The implementations are the oracle; see the shared-changes list for the format text. |
| L1-07 | `CharTokenizer.decode` writes a special's text unless `skip_special` is true (the L1.1 protocol); `formats/tokenizer.md` says decode skips the ids in `specials`. | Every tokenizer in the course follows the protocol's `skip_special` flag; see the shared-changes list. |
| L1-08 | Rung R2 learner tests: the chapter's section 4 gives the names and docstrings as a Python skeleton in a code block (not a table), and `ss start` writes no learner test file. Reference learner tests reach L1.1 0.95 (18/19), L1.2 0.72 (18/25), L1.3 1.00 (18/18), L1.4 0.67 (14/21), L1.6 0.92 (12/13) against the 0.60 bar. | The chapter lint reads every backticked first cell of a beat 4 table as a course test name, and 5.12 says the course provides names and docstrings with empty bodies. |
| L1-09 | Interfaces add to the catalog signatures: `check_ids`, `ByteTokenizer`, and `unk_id` in the protocol; `CharTokenizer.vocab/roles` and its constructor; `is_space`, `is_letter`, `is_number`, `split_digits`; `BPETokenizer.vocab/merges/added/pre_tokenizer` and its constructor; the WordPiece character classes, `normalize`, `wordpiece`, `from_hf_json`; `metaspace`, `viterbi`, `log_likelihood`, `em_step`, `from_hf_json`, `pieces`, `min_score` for Unigram; `is_fallback` for the metrics. `sample_encode` takes any object with `uniform()`. | The chapters' math (byte map, pre-tokenizer rules, EM by hand) needs each piece testable alone; L1.6 needs the protocol to say where unknown input goes. |
| L1-10 | The five build modules use `milestone = "MS-L1"`; `course/milestones/MS-L1.toml` is not written by this group (it needs L1.5 and the learner's `tok` CLI verbs). S-M07b uses `MS-P3`. | MS-L1 is not among this group's ids; the chapter lint checks "the milestone exercises the id" only once the file exists. |
| L1-11 | S-M07b has 9 numbered questions and 16 items: joint and covariance q1 to q4 (8 items), MLE q5 to q9 (8 items, q9 the proof), 15 checked by SymPy. `course/oracle/solve/S-M07b.py` recomputes all 15 independently (exact table sums, SymPy moment expansion, score equations, segmentation enumeration). The chapter is `math/07-probability-statistics/91-problem-set-b.md` with the problems block kept by `sync_chapters.py`. | Matches the 4.2 row (16 items, 1 rubric) and the solve conventions of SV05 and B41-12. |
| L1-12 | `pretok.py` and `wordpiece.py` write the non-ASCII White_Space characters as `\u` escapes, and the L1.1 to L1.4 mutants were regenerated after that change. | Raw U+2028 and U+2029 in a string literal broke the harness stubber (it splits lines like `str.splitlines`), so verify check 2 failed with "unterminated string literal", and difflib's line split shifted the patches. |
| L1-13 | Course tests run in about 1 s for all five modules together; over `SS_SEED` 0 to 30 none fails (the L1.4 chi-square tests included). | 4.0 budget and the false-alarm rate of the statistical rule. |

## B3 group B3-6: craft.03, L0.1, L0.2, L0.3, L0.4, L0.5, L0.6 (`B36`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B36-01 | craft.03's artifact is a kata, `primers/craft.03/failpoints.py` (the `TL_FAILPOINTS` spec the CLI evaluates in MS-L0, without the `P%` form) plus the learner's `test_failpoints.py`. Its check (`course/tests/craft.03/check`) runs the course's tests of the kata, then grades the learner's tests: black-box imports, green on their kata, green on `course/ref/primers/craft.03/failpoints.py`, and at least 0.70 of the 13 faults in `course/mutants/craft.03/` killed with `s01` required, one pytest process per fault in its own process group. Without a course tree (an exported repo) the two reference-kata steps skip with a message. The design's "`ss tdd` journal; mutation grade on L0 modules" is carried by L0.3 (R2) and L0.4 (R3, journal enforced by `ss check`). | Verify runs a practice check in a reference learner that has no ledger, so a check reading L0 verdicts could never pass there; the kata gives the mutation primer hands-on before the first graded module. |
| B36-02 | Rungs: L0.1, L0.2, L0.5, L0.6 are R0 (no `[learner_tests]`); L0.3 is R2 (threshold 0.60, no required mutant; the names and what each must show are in the chapter's section 4); L0.4 is R3 (0.70, `s01` required, red-then-green journal). Semantic mutants of R0 modules have `required = n`. | DESIGN 5.12 puts R1 ("fill the oracle") at L0.3+, but the harness has no R1 mechanism (M00-03); R2 and R3 are the nearest gradable rungs and give craft.03's ladder a first real grade. |
| B36-03 | L0.6 keeps L0.0's v0 signatures, `save_safetensors(path, tensors, meta)` and `load_safetensors(path) -> (tensors, meta)`, instead of DESIGN 4.3's `save_safetensors(tensors, path, metadata)` and a dict return; it adds `dtypes=` and `read_header`. BF16 and F8_E4M3 are written from float32 values (round to nearest, ties to even) or raw uint16/uint8 bits and load as float32 values; F8_E4M3 beyond 448 (or infinite) is a `ValueError`; float64 is never converted silently. | An upgrade keeps its contract (5.2): the Pass 1 CLI and engine keep working. numpy has no bfloat16 or float8 type, and the format table names their raw carriers. Saturation would hide a missing scale. |
| B36-04 | `save_checkpoint(dir, ...)` takes the `ckpt/` root and writes `step-<nnnnnn>/` plus `LATEST` (MSL0-08's assumption); it adds `keep=`. `extra` may hold only trainer_state.json fields plus `config` (written as config.json). The optimizer's non-array state (step count, hyperparameters) is JSON in `optimizer.safetensors`'s metadata key `state`; arrays are `<parameter>.<state key>`. `load_checkpoint` starts at what LATEST names, falls back to older complete directories, and ignores newer ones. | formats/checkpoint.md lists the metadata keys format, optimizer, step and says nothing of the scalars `load_state_dict` needs; a directory newer than LATEST was never declared done. |
| B36-05 | TokenStream's "random-window batches" are a random phase per pass over a shard, `rng.below(min(T, n - T))`, then non-overlapping windows `T` apart (each window's last id is the next one's first) until the next would pass the end; shards are read in turn. The cursor's offset is the next window's; the generator is drawn only at the start of a pass. TokenStream adds an optional `vocab_size`; the module adds `read_tokens_header` and `open_tokens`. | Every token after the phase is a target once per pass (uniform random windows leave about 37% unseen per epoch-equivalent), and the cursor stays a token offset as trainer-state.schema.json describes. |
| B36-06 | `BigramLogits.forward` accepts ids of any shape (the trainer feeds `[B, T]` windows). `BigramLM.sample` draws from `PCG32(seed)` (stream 54) with the L10.0 engine's arithmetic, not `stream(seed, "sample")` of K11. | Python must match the unchanged tracer engine, which predates the sub-stream rule (spec/pcg32.md says the same of L10.0). |
| B36-07 | L0.5 and L0.6 have `used_by = ["L10.0"]` (plus `L0.6` for L0.5) through the upgrade inheritance exemption; their catalog call sites (L2.1, L2.2, L3.6, L6.1, L7.9, L10.1, C1, dur.09) join with later batches. L0.6 lists L0.5 in `deps`. | The mirror rule accepts only registered modules. The harness runs L0.0's whole suite as L0.6's regression, and that suite covers `bigram.py`, which L0.5 owns: without L0.5 in the closure it ran against a stub. |
| B36-08 | Deps beyond the catalog's Prereqs, each a real call: L0.1 `M08.2` (the scalar oracle in its tests); L0.2 `M06.3`; L0.5 `rt.01` and `M03.1` (`BigramLM.logits` runs the C matmul), `M10.2`, `M10.3` (tests); L0.6 `L0.1`, `L0.2`, `L0.4`, `M10.2`, `M10.3`, `M06.3`. `lang.01`, `L0.0`, and `craft.03` are `reading`. | 3.4: deps are the modules whose code a module's units or course tests call. The owners' `used_by` mirrors are shared changes. |
| B36-09 | L0.6's golden files come from `course/oracle/L0.6/safetensors_golden.py` (PEP 723: safetensors 0.8.0 through its torch front end, torch 2.14.1, numpy 2.5.3), run offline from the uv cache. | As L00-06; numpy cannot hold BF16 or F8_E4M3, torch can. |
| B36-10 | `bigram.pyi` and `safetensors.pyi` now describe their current owners (L0.5, L0.6) and point to both chapters. L0.0's verify check 4 compares its history snapshot with them and fails (`BigramLogits`, `read_header`, `dtypes` missing). | The harness keeps one contract per unit at HEAD; the fix is in the harness (shared change). |
| B36-11 | L0.0's full suite runs as the regression of the units L0.5 and L0.6 take over; two of its assertions are v0-only (`load_safetensors` rejects F16, `save_safetensors` rejects float16 and int32) and fail against a correct L0.6. | Shared change to `course/tests/L0.0/test_safetensors.py` (use F64, int64, and bool, which no course writer or reader accepts); proven in a scratch copy of the course tree. |
| B36-12 | L0.5's learning tests read two rows of `course/fixtures/ref-thresholds.tsv`, recorded with `ss verify course L0.5 --record-thresholds` (5 seeds). | DESIGN 5.11, X10. |

## B5 group B5-3: M03.5, M03.6, S-M03b, M11.4, S-M11b, L2.1 (`B53`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B53-01 | `deps` hold only modules whose code is called: M03.5 `["M03.3"]` (`lstsq` factors with `qr_householder`), M03.6 `[]`, M11.4 `["M11.1"]` (`mutual_information` is `kl` of the joint against the product of its marginals), L2.1 `["M07.2", "M11.2", "L0.6"]` (`ney_discount`, `NLLAccumulator`, `save_safetensors`/`load_safetensors`). The catalog's other prereqs are `reading`: M03.4 for M03.5 (singular values as square roots of eigenvalues of A^T A; one-sided Jacobi calls no eigensolver), M03.1 for M03.6, M07.2 for M11.4. | Same rule as B41-03 and B33-02: a concept in `deps` would make the 3.4 mirror rule demand a call site in the prerequisite. |
| B53-02 | M03.5, M03.6, M11.4, and L2.1 have `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for all four. Their call sites are L2.3 (M03.5, M03.6, M11.4; another B5 group, not registered when this group was verified), and L6.7 and L8.6 (L2.1; B7, B8). Each joins `used_by` when that module's row lists it in `deps`. Until the upstream rows gain this group's ids (shared changes: M03.3 `used_by += ["M03.5"]`, M11.1 `+= ["M11.4"]`, M07.2 `+= ["L2.1"]`, M11.2 `+= ["L2.1"]`, L0.6 `+= ["L2.1"]`), verify check 13 also reports the mirror rule for those pairs. | The mirror rule accepts only registered callers, and this group may not edit other modules' rows. Inventing a caller would hide that the units are not used yet (M00-01, B41-04). |
| B53-03 | Contract additions to the 4.1 and 4.3 signatures: M03.5 pins the Jacobi rotation, the relative stopping test, a sign rule (largest entry of each `Vt` row positive), completion of `U` for zero singular values, the balanced split of `low_rank`, and `lstsq` raising on rank deficiency or `m < n`; M03.6 adds `normalize` and `project` and the D11 tie rule for `topk_cosine`; M11.4 adds `mutual_information`; L2.1 adds `vocab_size=` (None infers 1 + the largest id), `discounts(order)`, `nll(ids)`, `FALLBACK_DISCOUNTS`, the `<s>` and context-length rules, KenLM's fallback discounts `(0.5, 1.0, 1.5)`, and the save format (I32 `order{k}.grams` with `<s>` as -1 and `order{k}.counts`, metadata `tl_arch = "ngram"`). | The catalog signatures leave these choices open, and each one changes outputs a test or a later module compares bit for bit (LAPACK parity, the Go port of top-k, the zoo loading the file). |
| B53-04 | L2.1's golden values come from `course/oracle/L2.1/kn_golden.py`, an independent implementation in exact fractions (mpmath only for the final logs), on the worked-example corpus and on `course/fixtures/MS-P1/corpus.txt` read as bytes; no KenLM comparison. The design's TinyStories reference perplexity is MS-L2's job. | KenLM is not installed and building it would mean installing software; `small-corpora/tinyshakespeare.txt` was never added (I42). The MS-P1 corpus is committed, original, and small. |
| B53-05 | The n-gram model is one safetensors file with `tl_arch = "ngram"` in its metadata, not a model directory with `config.json`; `"ngram"` is not yet in the `tl_arch` enums of `formats/config.schema.json` and `formats/eval-results.schema.json`. | `NGramLM.save(path)` takes a file path (4.3). Adding the enum value is a contracts change (shared). |
| B53-06 | Solve chapters are `math/03-linear-algebra/91-problem-set-b.md` and `math/11-information-theory/91-problem-set-b.md` (after S-M06b's naming); M11.4's chapter is `04-mutual-information-pmi-ppmi.md`, leaving `03` to the optional M11.3. S-M11b also carries the solve-only topics M11.5 (maximum entropy, temperature) and M11.6 (rate-distortion), and the coding-theorem items behind M11.3. Item counts match 4.2: S-M03b 16 (2 proofs), S-M11b 22 (3 proofs). | 4.1 marks M11.5 and M11.6 "solve only" and 4.2 lists their topics in S-M11b. |
| B53-07 | `topk_cosine` scores each query through `cosine_sim` (an elementwise product summed per row), not a BLAS matrix product. | A matrix product can round identical rows differently in different blocks, which would break the lowest-index tie rule at random. |

Quantiles are bisection to the last bit; the t CDF is the Abramowitz and Stegun 26.7.3/26.7.4 closed form, O(df). The bootstrap's index rule (`min(floor(u n), n - 1)`, one uniform per index, resample-major) and the type 7 quantile are pinned so ag.12 can reproduce intervals in Go. `wilson_interval` returns exactly 0.0 at k = 0 and 1.0 at k = n. | "Every metric carries a CI" (L6.7) needs correct coverage at small n; no scipy in learner units; cross-language reproducibility. |

| B6M-05 | S-M07c has 9 numbered questions and 10 items (q1 has parts a and b): 9 checked by SymPy and q5 (the weak law of large numbers via Chebyshev) a proof. Chapter `math/07-probability-statistics/92-problem-set-c.md`; reference answers `course/ref/solve/S-M07c.toml` and `S-M07c/q5.md`; `course/oracle/solve/S-M07c.py` confirms 9 of 9 independently. | DESIGN 4.2 (LLN/CLT 6, intervals 4, 1 rubric); SV01 to SV03 naming. |
| B6M-06 | L3.1's interface adds `RNNCache` (a NamedTuple: x, h0, h, Wxh, Whh), `tbptt_grads`, and `gradient_flow` (the exploding-gradient diagnostic, the M03.4 call site) to the catalog's three functions. Convention: row vectors, `Wxh [D, H]`, `Whh [H, H]`, one bias (torch's `weight_ih_l0 = Wxh.T`, `bias = b_ih + b_hh`). A TBPTT window ending at e starts at max(0, e - k2) and differentiates the losses since the previous cut. | The catalog names truncation and the rho(W_hh) property but gives no API for either; L3.6 needs the conventions fixed. |
| B6M-07 | L3.2 `LSTM` and L3.3 `GRU` have no `bidirectional` argument (the catalog lists one): a bidirectional layer is L3.4's `bidirectional(fwd, bwd, x, lengths)` over two single-direction modules, torch's `*_l0` and `*_l0_reverse` weights loaded into the two. Both gain `rng` (L0.4's convention) and the functional cells `lstm_cell` and `gru_cell`; `LSTMCell.forward(x, state)`, `GRUCell.forward(x, h)`. | A `bidirectional=True` inside L3.2/L3.3 needs L3.4's length-aware reversal, which would make L3.2 and L3.4 depend on each other. |
| B6M-08 | LSTM/GRU initialization is not torch's U(-1/sqrt(H), 1/sqrt(H)): per gate block, `xavier_uniform` (M07.3) for w_ih and `orthogonal_init` (M03.3) for w_hh; the LSTM forget-gate block of b_ih is 1. The torch parity tests load torch's weights, so they are unaffected. | The catalog lists M07.3 and M03.3 as call sites of the recurrent init; forget bias 1 is the documented practice (Gers 2000, Jozefowicz 2015). |
| B6M-09 | `lengths` in `LSTM.forward`/`GRU.forward` is a mask equivalent to torch's `pack_padded_sequence`: past its length a sequence carries its state unchanged and outputs 0, so `h_n` is the state after its last real step. `T >= 1` is required. `bidirectional` returns only the output, as the catalog; the final states are documented as `out[lengths - 1, b, :H_f]` and `out[0, b, H_f:]`. | One masking rule shared by the RNN modules, ELMo (L3.5), and the seq2seq encoder (L4.1). |
| B6M-10 | `deps` follow code calls: L3.1 `["M08.3", "M03.4", "L0.1", "L0.2"]` (L0.1 and L0.2 because the course tests compare manual BPTT with your autograd), L3.2 and L3.3 `["L0.1", "L0.2", "L0.4", "M07.3", "M03.3", "M06.3"]`, L3.4 `["L3.2", "L3.3", "L0.1", "L0.2"]`. The manifest `required` column is `y` only for the required mutants at R3, because the grader treats every `y` as required. | DESIGN 5.12 (R3 "1 semantic", R4 "all semantic"). |

## B5 group B5-4: L2.2, L2.3, MS-L2 (`B54`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B54-01 | MS-L2, L2.2's learning test, and L2.3's tests use a synthetic stand-in for TinyStories: `course/fixtures/MS-L2/{train,val}.bin` (141 532 and 14 675 byte tokens, formats/tokens-bin.md version 1, `vocab_size` 256; 560 and 60 disjoint stories) generated by the stdlib script `course/oracle/MS-L2/corpus.py` from a fixed grammar and a PCG32, plus the word-similarity gold pairs `course/fixtures/L2.3/wordsim.json` (611 pairs; 3 same class, 1 same part of speech, 0 otherwise) from the same class table. A grammar with class-specific slots gives word2vec real distributional signal and a gold standard with a known license (WordSim-353 and SimLex-999 are not committed). Regenerating reproduces all three files byte for byte. |

"Within 0.5%" is two bounds, `ppl <= 1.005 r` and `nll_mean >= ln(0.995 r)`, with `r = 1.8699475121183176176` from the exact-fraction oracle `course/oracle/MS-L2/kn4_ref.py` (the reference CLI prints the same value to 16 digits). "Below the bigram" is `nll_mean < 2.142401587`, L0.0's add-one bigram on the same files (`corpus.py`). | `eval-result` describes per-case rows of agent suites (ag.09), and the json-last-line matcher takes one bound per metric. `course/oracle/L2.1/kn_golden.py` scans every n-gram per query and is too slow for 140 KB. |

## B6 group B6-2: L3.5, L3.6, L4.1, L4.2, L4.3, L4.4 (`B62`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B62-01 | L3.5 (ELMo) is registered as `kind = "side"`, pass 4, `milestone = ""`, with `used_by = []`. | D31 makes it optional with only a side-quest call site (`sq.elmo-probes`); a `build` module with no registered call site fails verify check 9, and `side` is the registry kind for code with no call site. Its deps' `used_by` need no mirror entry (the 3.4 rule skips side modules). |
| B62-02 | L3.5's linear probe is its own softmax regression by full-batch gradient descent (`fit_linear_probe`, `probe_accuracy`), not `M07.7`'s IRLS logistic regression; `M07.7` is not a dep. | M07.7 is taught in Pass 5 (B7), after L3.5's pass, so the 3.4 pass-order rule forbids the dep; the chapter names M07.7 as the catalog's probe. |
| B62-03 | L3.6 and L4.4 have `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for both until L6.7 (B7) lists them in `deps` (the zoo's `rnnlm` rows and beam decoding). L4.1's catalog call site L6.7 is also unregistered; L4.4's course tests beam-search a `Seq2Seq`, which makes `L4.4` a real call site of L4.1 (and of L4.3, whose Luong state the search gathers). | The mirror rule accepts only registered callers; same situation as B53-02 and B41-04. |
| B62-04 | `deps` list every module whose code a unit or a course test calls, foundations included (L0.1 to L0.6, M03.3, M06.3, M07.3, M10.3, L1.1, L3.1 to L3.4). Until the owners' `used_by` rows gain this group's ids (shared changes), verify check 13 reports the mirror rule for those pairs. | B36-08's rule; this group may not edit other modules' rows. |
| B62-05 | L4.4's beam narrows as hypotheses finish: a step keeps `beam_size - finished` candidates, and the search stops when `beam_size` have finished. The contract adds `Hypothesis.finished`, `select_state`, and `greedy_decode` to the catalog's signature, and pins the op order (log-softmax per row, one ranking over all k * V candidates, ties to the lower flat index). | A fixed-width beam that refills after each `eos` never stops before `max_len`; the narrowing beam terminates, keeps `beam = 1` equal to greedy and `beam >= V ** max_len` equal to exhaustive search (both tested), and is what Graves (2012) describes. A state is a pytree of arrays gathered by parent so any model's decoder state works. |
| B62-06 | L4.1's encoder is `L3.4`'s `bidirectional` over two GRUs of width `d_h / 2` (so `d_h` must be even), and the decoder's first state is `tanh(bridge([fwd final ; bwd final]))`. The catalog signature gains `rng`, `init_state`, `greedy`, `EncoderState`/`DecoderState` NamedTuples, and `save_seq2seq`/`load_seq2seq` with `config.json` keys `tl_src_vocab`, `tl_seq2seq_attention`, `tl_d_attn` (not yet in `formats/config.schema.json`, which allows unknown keys). | Keys of width `d_h` let Luong's `dot` score compare the decoder state with the encoder outputs directly; the zoo (L6.7) loads every family through a model directory, as L2.2 did for the NPLM. |
| B62-07 | Attention modules carry a class attribute `query_from` (`"previous"` for Bahdanau, `"current"` for Luong) that the decoder reads to choose the order of attention and recurrence; both modules share `project_keys`, `scores`, and `forward(query, keys, mask, proj)`, and the mask is boolean with True = may be read (`length_mask`, owned by L4.2). Luong's `attentional` provides `h~`; input feeding itself is the decoder's concatenation (L4.1). | One plug-in interface for both papers; L4.3's unit holds the score functions and `W_c`, L4.1 the data flow. |
| B62-08 | L3.6 adds to the catalog: `ElmanRNN` (torch.nn.RNN names, one `from_op` node per layer over L3.1's `rnn_forward`/`rnn_backward`), `tbptt_batches`, `init_state`, `detach_state`, `nll`, `generate`, `save_rnnlm`/`load_rnnlm`. Token arrays are batch first (`[B, T]`) while recurrent states keep the cells' `[n_layers, B, d_h]`. `train_tbptt` runs each window through L0.5's `train_step` and resets the state at each epoch. | The catalog says `cell='rnn'` is L3.1's backward as one fused op; the rest is what MS-L3's CLI and the zoo need. |
| B62-09 | L3.6's learning test uses `course/fixtures/L3.6/corpus.txt` (23 910 bytes, an original synthetic CC0 text from the stdlib generator `course/oracle/L3.6/corpus.py`), 150 steps of a 64-wide character LSTM, not the catalog's 200 KB fixture and 300 steps. L4.1's learning tests train the reversal task generated in the test (150 steps each, with and without attention: exact match about 0.94 against 0.4); L3.5's trains on a two-sentence-type grammar generated in the test. | The tinyshakespeare fixture was never added (I42); the reference finishes 150 steps in 0.6 to 3 s depending on machine load, which keeps `ss verify course L3.6` (21 course-test runs and 16 learner-test runs) inside the 120 s per-process cap of this batch; at 150 steps the LSTM reaches 0.76 bpc against 0.84 for the Elman RNN. |
| B62-10 | Golden fixtures are torch 2.14.1 forward and backward values with copied weights from PEP 723 scripts (`course/oracle/L3.5`, `L4.1`, `L4.2`, `L4.3`): torch has no Bahdanau or Luong module, so their formulas are written with torch ops; L4.1 uses a bidirectional `nn.GRU` over `pack_padded_sequence` and `GRUCell`/`LSTMCell` via `torch._VF`. Committed fixtures of this group: 130 574 bytes. | Same pattern as B34-08 and B54-12; the rest of the B6 budget (3 MiB) is left to the other group. |
| B62-11 | Rungs: L3.6 is R4 (0.80, s01 s05 s06 s07 s08 required), L4.1 to L4.4 are R5 (0.80, every pitfall mutant required); L3.5 has no `[learner_tests]` (optional side module). Reference learner tests: L4.4 19/19, L4.2 14/14, L4.3 13/13, L4.1 17/17, L3.6 13/14 (`m04`, the skipped last window, survives). | 5.12 places L3 at R3/R4 and L4 at R5; R5's oracles here are brute force (L4.4), the formulas in numpy (L4.2, L4.3), and the model's own pieces recombined (L4.1). |
| B62-12 | Every test's `CATCHES` list was computed from a kill matrix (each committed mutant run against the full course suite, one at a time), not written by hand; mutants found equivalent were dropped (L4.4 `s12`: the result never exceeds `beam_size` in a narrowing beam). | 5.12: nightly verify proves each claim; a measured matrix makes the claims true by construction. |
| B62-13 | Not written by this group: the `<!-- ss:chapters -->` blocks of `ml/08-tinyllm/p03-recurrent/README.md` and `p04-attention-origins/README.md` (`ss lint --fix-index`, track index files), the MS-L3 and MS-L4 milestone specs and pages (other B6 group), and `course/ref/entry` CLI verbs (`train rnnlm`, `train seq2seq`, `translate`). | Shared files and other owners. |
| B62-14 | L3.6 commits 14 mutants (two weak ones, the dropped last window and the swapped targets, were removed after the kill check: both duplicated `s01`'s window coverage), its gradcheck runs the Elman and LSTM cells only, and its learning test runs 150 steps; L4.1's learning tests run 150 steps. | Each `ss verify course <ID>` of this batch ran as one process under a 120 s wall-clock cap with mutants run one at a time; with the harness's per-run overhead (a fresh `uv run` and bytecode cache per overlay, about 2 to 3 s at load 8) the first L3.6 attempts were killed at 120 s. Final runs: L3.6 94.4 s, L4.1 109.2 s. |

### B7-4: M05.1, L7.1 to L7.5 (counting, RMSNorm, gated MLPs, RoPE, context extension, GQA)

| B71-04 | Contracts add to the 4.1/4.3 signatures: `trapezoid_rule` (M01.4); `mean_difference`, `holm_adjust`, and `mcnemar(..., exact=True)` (M07.5); `logistic_predict_proba`, `roc_curve`, `reliability_bins`, and `fit_intercept=True` with the intercept LAST (M07.7, the layout L6.5 writes into `linear-head`'s `b`); `sdpa_forward` and `sdpa_backward` (L5.1). `scaled_dot_product_attention` returns `(out, weights)` with `weights` a constant Tensor (P before dropout); `padding_mask` returns `[B, T]` (index it `[:, None, None, :]` for keys). | Each addition is a seam a test or a caller uses: the rule's order test, the zoo's adjusted p column, the head export, the C kernel's oracle (L9.3), and L5.3's per-head weights. |
| B71-05 | scikit-learn, the design's oracle for M07.7, is not available offline; the M07.7 fixture comes from scipy (BFGS on the same objective, `mannwhitneyu` for the AUC) and exact Fractions for the ECE bins. The scipy oracles of this group run with `uv run --offline --python 3.11 --script`, the interpreter of the cached scipy 1.17.1 environment. | No software installs; independent routes keep the oracle meaningful. |
| B71-06 | Permutation tests (M07.5) are two-sided on `|stat|`, return the add-one estimate `(1 + count) / (1 + n_perm)`, count ties within a relative 1e-9, draw only through `rng.uniform()` (one per pair; Fisher-Yates from the end, restarted from the identity each permutation), so load.02's Go port can match p-values bit for bit. | The catalog fixes the functions, not these rules; each is pinned by a test (replay golden, draw counts, the tie case). |
| B71-07 | McNemar's chi-square variant uses `max(|b01 - b10| - 1, 0)^2 / n`; statsmodels gives `1 / n` (p < 1) when b01 = b10. The exact variant is the default. | A tie is no evidence; p = 1 there matches the exact test. |
| B71-08 | ECE (M07.7) is top-label (Guo et al. 2017): binary prediction `p >= 0.5`, confidence `max(p, 1 - p)`; bins `(b/B, (b+1)/B]` by `ceil(c B) - 1`, clipped to `[0, B - 1]`. | The design names `ece(probs, labels, n_bins)` without its convention; this is the paper's and is pinned by the bin-edge test. |
| B71-09 | A fully masked SDPA row returns zero weights and output (torch's SDPA returns NaN there); the L5.1 golden cases have no fully masked row. | M09.2's softmax defines that row as zeros, and padded queries of empty sequences must not poison gradients. |
| B71-10 | Rungs: M01.4, M07.5, M07.7 have no `[learner_tests]` (math, as M01.1 and M07.4); L5.1 and L5.2 are R5 at 0.80 with every semantic mutant required. Reference learner tests reach 17/17 (L5.1) and 12/14 (L5.2). Two course tests kill no committed mutant and are tagged smoke: `test_trapezoid_short_inputs` (M01.4) and `test_key_permutation_equivariance` (L5.1, the catalog's required property). | 5.12 puts L4 to L7 at R5; no plausible single fault breaks only those two properties. |

### B7-2: L5.3 to L5.5, MS-L5, L6.1, L6.2 (multi-head attention, positions, the 2017 transformer, GPT, BERT)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B72-01 | MS-L5 trains on `small-corpora/add3.tsv` (4000 pairs) and decodes `add3-test.tsv` (500 unseen), three-digit addition written least significant digit first ("321+654" -> "9750"), not the catalog's `add5.tsv`. Reference: 3500 updates of a pre-LN 1 + 1 layer d = 64 model (`configs/xfmr-small.json`) give exact match 1.000 with beam 4 (0.978 after 2500). | Five-digit addition, in either digit order, stayed above 0.2 nats per token after 6000 numpy-autograd updates (about 105 s), and no verification run may exceed 120 s here. Reversed digits are the standard trick (Lee et al. 2023): the decoder writes the ones digit first and carries forward. |
| B72-02 | MS-L5 does not report `em_seq2seq_luong` (the L4 model on the same task). | That needs MS-L4's `train seq2seq` glue (B6) on add3; `add3.tsv` uses the same `source<TAB>target` format, so MS-L4's owner can add a report-only step. |
| B72-03 | The post-LN demo defines "diverged" as a non-finite loss or `loss_ratio` (mean of the last 20 updates over the first 10) above 0.8, on a 6 + 6 layer d = 64 model (`configs/xfmr-deep.json`) at a constant 3e-3 for 150 updates. Reference: post-LN 0.91 (it learns the digit frequencies only), pre-LN 0.54. The CLI prints both `"diverged": 0/1` (the numeric metric the matcher compares) and `"post_ln_no_warmup_diverged": true/false`. | At this size post-LN without warmup stalls rather than producing NaN (Xiong et al. report the same failure to train); `json-last-line` compares numbers only. |

L5.4: `sinusoidal_freqs`, `shift_pe` (the rotation law, through M00.2), `offset` on every encoding, `SinusoidalPE`, `LearnedPE.positions`, `std`, `rng`. L5.5: `EncoderLayer`, `DecoderLayer`, `DecodeState`, `embed`, `init_state`, `label_smoothed_loss`, `noam_rate`, `make_optimizer`, `fit`, `translate`, `save_transformer`, `load_transformer`, and `max_len`/`ln_eps` in the config; `src_mask`/`tgt_mask` are padding masks and the model builds causality itself. L6.1: `Block`, `GPT.hidden`, `fit_gpt`, `save_gpt`, `load_gpt`, `INIT_STD`. | B73-10 | Strided perplexity requires `1 <= stride < ctx_len` and scores every token 1..n-1 exactly once from the first window that reaches it; the HF golden is computed under that definition (`course/oracle/L6.7/strided_ppl_hf.py`), not with the HF docs recipe, which leaves each window's first target unscored when stride equals the window and miscounts tokens. `eval_ppl`'s interval is M07.4's Student t on per-token NLLs; the zoo uses Wilson for proportions and Fisher z for Spearman; only `run_task` bootstraps. | A bootstrap over 10^5 tokens with a Python PCG32 is too slow for a test or a CLI call. |

| B73-12 | ELECTRA masks with L6.2's `mlm_mask` (BERT's 80/10/10) instead of the original code's 85% [MASK] / 15% kept, samples replacements with M07.1's inverse CDF (one uniform per picked position) instead of Gumbel noise, and shrinks the generator by layers and d_ff only (equal d_model, so the embeddings can be tied). | One masking op order for the course (B72-05); inverse CDF is the sampler of D11; tying needs equal widths. |
| B73-13 | Mutant patches are unified diffs against the marker-dropped reference (what verify check 6 applies them to). Kill matrices and the CATCHES lines were computed one mutant at a time in a guarded runner outside the harness, then confirmed by `ss verify course`; learning thresholds (L6.5) and MS-L6's calibrated bars were recorded over seeds 0 to 4 with `sscourse.thresholds`, one guarded process per run. | The task's runner rules (one mutant at a time, 120 s and 4 GB per process group); `ss milestone --record-thresholds` runs every seed in one process. |
| B73-14 | Committed fixtures of this group: 261 KiB (L6.3 36 248 B, L6.4 4 916 B, L6.5 3 602 B, L6.7 41 016 B, small-corpora/sst2-2k.tsv 78 805 B, MS-L6/sst2-train.bin 102 492 B, configs 422 B). | Within B7's 8 MiB. |

## B7 group B7-5: L7.6, L7.7, L7.8, L7.9, craft.05, MS-L7 (`B75`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B75-01 | The per-module contracts are new files of this group: `py/tinyllm/modern/{mla,window,moe,llama}.pyi` and `py/tinyllm/io/hf.pyi`. Beyond DESIGN 4.3's signatures: `MLAttention` takes `attention_bias`, `softmax_scale`, `rng`; L7.6 adds `LatentCacheHook`, `ConcatLatentCache`, and pins `AbsorbedMLA` (`w_uk`, `w_ov`, `o_bias`); L7.7 adds `sink_window_mask`, `windowed_attention` (with lse), `attention_mask`, and `SinkWindowCache.{seq_len,held,positions,chunk_mask}`; L7.8 adds `topk_ids`, `routed_scaling`, `act`, `rng`, and `aux_loss`/`expert_load` as read-only properties; L7.9 adds `LlamaConfig` fields for every extension, `to_hf`, `model_config`, `rope_spec`, `LlamaForCausalLM(cfg, rng, init)`, `aux_loss`, `param_count`, `save_pretrained`; `hf_download` adds `endpoint`, `sha256`, `timeout`. | K01: per-module language contracts arrive with their module; the additions are what the tests, L7.9, and the CLI need to call. |
| B75-02 | DESIGN 4.3's optional `mha_to_mla` is not built; M03.5 is `reading` for L7.6 (the chapter uses the low-rank view to explain the latent). | GQA rotates whole heads while MLA rotates only a shared rope key, and MLA's latent RMSNorm is not linear, so no exact conversion exists (TransMLA and MHA2MLA fine-tune after an SVD initialization). |
| B75-03 | `SinkWindowCache` keeps keys as the attention layer hands them over (rotated at absolute positions), so attention through it equals full attention under the sink-window mask; StreamingLLM's re-rotation of keys at their cache slots is a Going-further pointer. L7.9 builds `GQAttention(window=None)` and passes L7.7's `attention_mask`, so sink tokens are exempt from the window. | An exact, testable equivalence (E: vs full attention + mask); L7.5's own `window` cannot exempt sink tokens. |
| B75-04 | MoE experts are per-expert `GatedMLP`s (`experts.<e>.{gate,up,down}_proj.weight`, the DeepSeek and Qwen Hub layout), not transformers 5's fused `gate_up_proj`; the oracle splits HF's fused tensors. The load-balancing loss is HF's `load_balancing_loss_func` (k at perfect balance), not Switch's normalization to 1. The aux-free bias is plain state, not in `state_dict`. Mixtral Hub checkpoints (`block_sparse_moe.*`, `w1/w2/w3`) are not renamed on load. | L7.2's `GatedMLP` is the expert call site the design names; HF is the golden oracle; L0.4 has no buffers and an optimizer must not decay the bias; MoE checkpoints in the course are the learner's own (C1). |

Its catalog call sites (L8.1 to L8.6, L9.7, L10.1, C1, the L6.7 zoo's llama rows) are not registered with L7.9 in `deps`; the registered L8.2 depends on L7.5 and L7.3 only. | The mirror rule accepts only registered modules (same situation as B53-02 and B74-05); shared change: L8.2 (or L6.7) lists L7.9 in deps. |
| B75-06 | L7.9 `deps` are the modules llama.py calls (L7.1 to L7.8, L0.6, M05.1, L0.4, L0.2, L0.1, M06.3); M09.1 and L1.2 are `reading`. | L0.6's reader decodes BF16 (with M09.1's converters); the SmolLM2 tokenizer is used only by the CLI glue of MS-L7. |
| B75-07 | MS-L7's nightly steps and the `smollm2-parity` fixture use SmolLM2-135M-Instruct at its pinned revision (`smollm2-135m-instruct` in ASSETS.tsv), not the base SmolLM2-135M. | Same architecture and parameter count (134,515,008); its weights were already in the local Hugging Face cache, so the fixture was generated offline and the steps were run for real (pull, info, 256 greedy ids exact, logits max abs 6.0e-5 with identical top-5). MS-agent uses the same model later (D20). |
| B75-08 | No milestone matcher compares a logits `.npz`: MS-L7's PR steps compare the tiny model's 256 next-token logits with `numeric` (values inline, atol 1e-3, the design's SmolLM2 bound); the nightly `smollm2-logits` step uses the `logits-close` matcher added later (the 256 reference logits of `logits_top.npz` within 1e-3, top-5 ids identical). | `numeric` and `tokens-equal` were the matchers that existed when MS-L7 was written. |
| B75-09 | L7.9's golden logits tolerance is rtol 1e-5 with atol 1e-5 times the largest reference logit (about 2e-4). | The random tiny models give logits up to about 20, so float32 rounding scales with them; 5.11's "tiny HF models atol 1e-5" assumes unit-scale logits. |

Building SmolLM2 this way takes 0.4 s. |

Its milestone is MS-P5. | The design's artifact ("differential tests against fixtures for L5 to L7; must kill the transposed grad mutant") on hand-written backwards, where transposed gradients happen; the craft.04 pattern. |

M07.1's `sample_categorical` already returns the largest id with `q > 0`. |

KVCache also has `positions`, `mask` (L5.2's offset causal mask), and `nbytes`; `LatentCache` implements L7.6's hook. `generate` adds `eos_ids`, `Generation.finish_reason`, and accepts a cache object (L8.3's) where the catalog had `cache="paged"`; the string `"paged"` is a ValueError. A request without a seed uses seed 0. | L7.5's `ConcatKVCache`, L7.7's `attention_mask`, and L7.9's forward call `cache.seq_len(layer)`; the catalog's `"paged"` mode would make L8.2 import L8.3, which depends on L8.2. |
| B81-07 | L8.2's course tests drive test-local models (`course/tests/L8.2/_tinylm.py`: a two-layer GQA decoder with RoPE, and a scripted model) plus one test each through L7.9's `LlamaForCausalLM` and L7.5's `GQAttention`. `cache_dims` reads a model's `n_layers`/`n_kv_heads`/`d_head`/`max_len` or its `config` (or `cfg`) with LlamaConfig names. | A failure must point at the cache and the loop; the L7.9 and L7.5 tests prove the seams the milestone uses. |

Acceptance is `u < p_x / q_x` (one float64 division, vLLM's form); sums are left-to-right in ascending ids. | The module is titled for both rejection sampling and residuals; L8.6 and L10.8 need one bit-exact acceptance rule. |

M07.6, M09.3, M09.4 have none (math modules, as M09.2). ### B8-5: L9.7, craft.06, MS-L9 (the Python C backend, benchmarks and perf gates, the kernels milestone)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B85-01 | L9.7's `used_by` is empty: its one catalog call site, L10.1 (the Rust runner compared with `--backend c`), is in B9. Verify check 9 fails for L9.7 until L10.1 lists it in `deps`. Check 13 also reports `rt.01` and `L7.9` missing L9.7 in `used_by`; those rows belong to other groups. | The mirror rule accepts only registered callers (B41-04, B81-03). Shared change: add `"L9.7"` to `used_by` of `course/modules/rt.01.toml` and `course/modules/L7.9.toml`. |
| B85-02 | MS-L9 step (2) gates `tok_s_c >= 0.001 * decode_tok_s` (calibration-relative) and only reports `speedup_c`, instead of DESIGN 4.3's `speedup_c >= 3`. The local step runs on SmolLM2-135M-Instruct (as MS-L7, B75-07). | Measured here: a SmolLM2-sized model (synthetic weights, one thread) decodes at 77 tok/s through numpy (Accelerate BLAS) and 6.9 tok/s through the reference C kernels; on tiny-llama-2l `speedup_c` is 0.82. numpy's matmul is the platform BLAS, so "3x numpy" measures the BLAS, not the learner. One SmolLM2 token is about 134M MACs, 128 calibration GEMV steps; 0.001 x 2762 steps/s = 2.8 tok/s, below the reference's 6.9. |
| B85-03 | L9.7 `[learner_tests]` is rung R7 at 0.90 with unit mutants only (23: 17 semantic, 6 auto); the perf-mutant half of R7 is graded on craft.06's kata. | Perf mutants of a Python backend over a ctypes boundary cost under 2x on the tiny models and are timing-flaky on a shared machine; craft.06 grades the same skill on kernels where every planted slowdown is 2x to 800x. |
| B85-04 | craft.06 is `ci = "local"`: its check times benchmarks. `ss verify course craft.06` still runs it (passing here in 95 s with the whole reference learner assembled). | DESIGN 5.14: perf gates are local only. The gate compares base and head on one machine, so it holds on any machine, but CI runners are shared. |
| B85-05 | MS-L9 step (3) ("the course C harness for every L9 and rt module is sanitizer-clean") is the milestone's `requires` (rt.02, rt.03, L9.1 to L9.6), not a step: a fresh pass of each ran its course C tests under ASan and UBSan with the counting allocator. | A milestone step runs a learner entry role; no role runs the course's C harness. |
| B85-06 | craft.06 has `units = []`: the check writes `primers/craft.06/kernels.h` and a stubbed `kernels.c` on its first run (as craft.07). | The precheck (`precheck.c_unit`) rejects a C unit whose exported names (`kata_*`) no contract header declares, and a C primer is not under `c/`. |
| B85-07 | craft.06's planted faults are loop order (ijk), 2-column tiles, duplicated work (matmul, rmsnorm), one accumulator (sum, rmsnorm), `volatile` partial sums, an O(d^2) norm, a linear search, and a defensive copy; DESIGN 5.12's "tiling disabled" is not one. The gate compares base and head kernels.c alternately (no stored numbers), and the budget file holds one max-regression fraction per metric. | Measured here: the whole matrix as one tile costs 1.4x at n = 256 (it fits in cache), below any sane budget; every committed fault costs 2x (duplicated work) to 800x and keeps the results correct (`kata_check.c`). The full tiles go through `axpy_tile` (fixed trip count, `restrict` parameters) because gcc at -O2 vectorizes only that shape: with the plain `min_i` inner loop the Linux reference was scalar and loop order (s01, required) cost only 1.4x, against 10x now. |
| B85-08 | L9.7 imports rt.01's `STATUS` marker as `signatures()["tl_matmul_f32"][0]` rather than `from tinyllm.ffi.libtinyllm import STATUS`. | Verify check 14 reads only functions and classes from a `.pyi` (`precheck._api`), so a module constant such as `STATUS` counts as undeclared. Shared change: let `_api` record module-level `AnnAssign` names. |
| B85-09 | The L9.7 op check rounds the RoPE angle to float before cos and sin, as `tinyllm/elementwise.h` and the L9.6 reference do; its bounds and the test tolerances are those of `c.pyi`. Golden tests compare logits within 1e-4 of their range, ten times tighter than MS-L9's 1e-3. | On the tiny checkpoints an eps of 1e-5 or a mis-scaled bound hides inside 1e-3; the large-eps differential case catches the eps mutant. |
| B85-10 | No committed fixture was added: L9.7 and MS-L9 reuse L7.9's tiny checkpoints, `hf_logits.npz`, `margin-prompts.txt`, `tiny_greedy_32.json`, and `smollm2-parity/`. SmolLM2 weights were not downloaded; MS-L9's nightly and local steps were not run (the local step's budget was checked on a synthetic SmolLM2-sized model). | Fixture budget; network rule. |

### B8-2: ds.02, ds.03, rt.04, L8.3, ds.07, L8.4, L8.5 (Swiss table, intrusive LRU, paged KV block pool, paged cache, radix tree, radix prefix cache, quantization)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B82-01 | ds.07's `radix.rs` needs `pub mod radix;` in the tl-ds crate root `rust/crates/tl-ds/src/lib.rs`, which ds.05 owns; this group did not edit it (shared change). ds.07 and L8.4 were verified against a mirrored course tree (`SS_COURSE_ROOT`) whose only difference is that line. | The file belongs to ds.05; its own comment says the radix tree joins as one more `pub mod` line. |
| B82-02 | ds.02, ds.03, and rt.04 list `contract = []`; their cards link `tinyllm/ds.h`, `tinyllm/kv_pool.h`, and `formats/kv-block.md`. | Those files carry no `chapter:` back-pointer, which `ss lint` requires of a listed contract (as B81-02, M063-02). |
| B82-03 | The ds.02 chapter fixes what `ds.h` leaves open: the hash is the SplitMix64 finalizer, H2 = low 7 bits, H1 = the rest; groups probe triangularly; `tl_map_create(hint)` and every rebuild size for `cap_for(n)` = smallest 16 * 2^k with n <= 7/8 of it (never below create's size); a delete writes EMPTY when its group still has an EMPTY slot, else DELETED. Course tests use the hash only to pick hard keys; their assertions hold for any hash. | The 7/8 boundary and tombstone tests need a defined sizing rule; the EMPTY-in-group rule is what lets rt.04 churn its index without allocating. |
| B82-04 | ds.02's `[bench]` budget is `miss_speedup >= 1.3` (3,071 keys in 4,096 slots, load 3/4 for both tables), not the catalog's "hit lookup >= 1.3x". Measured on this machine (Apple arm64, -O2): hits 1.03 to 1.05x, misses 2.1 to 3.2x the linear-probing baseline. | A SWAR hit costs one compare like a linear-probing hit; the Swiss table's win is the miss, which is the prefix index's common case. |
| B82-05 | L8.4 `used_by = []` (verify check 9 fails) until L10.4 registers with L8.4 in `deps`; B9's `block_manager.rs` already calls `RadixCache`. | The mirror rule accepts only registered call sites. |
| B82-06 | ds.03: `tinyllm/ds.h` has no list operations besides the four LRU calls (no splice), so the catalog's "splice" test is replaced by a safe-iteration test and touch-as-move tests; `list.c` holds `tl_lru_init`, `tl_lru_remove`, `tl_lru_pop_oldest`, `lru.c` holds `tl_lru_touch`. | The header is frozen (not this group's to edit); each unit still owns real functions. |
| B82-07 | rt.04 choices the header leaves open: export of a block with fill 0 is `TL_EINVAL` (reader rule 5 would refuse it); the free list is a `tl_vec` reserved for `n_blocks` at create and popped by reading the last id and shortening `len` (`ds.h` makes the fields public); `tl_kv_register` can return `TL_ENOMEM` if the index must rebuild and the hook fails; an import that hits that error unrefs every block it took. | ds.01's catalog call site is rt.04; the rest keeps the pool allocation-free after create. |
| B82-08 | L8.3 contract choices: `append(seq, layer, k, v)` takes `[n_kv_heads, T, d_head]` (no batch axis); layers append separately and a block's fill is the positions every layer holds; `OutOfBlocks` is raised before any visible change; `pool` exposes the `tl_kv_pool *` for L9.4. `paged.py` declares status-returning functions with a plain `c_int32` result and maps `TL_EFULL` (5) itself, instead of importing `STATUS`/`TL_EFULL` from the loader. | Verify check 14 reads only functions and classes from a `.pyi`, so module constants of `libtinyllm.pyi` count as undeclared (B85-08 hit the same). |

The order is still fixed per output element, so rule 10 holds (`batch_invariant_rows`). | One accumulator chain made the int4 GEMV slower than the float32 matmul (1.75 ms vs 4.0 ms per step was the best single-chain version); with lanes held in registers it is 0.99 ms, 3.9x float32 (budget >= 2). ## B9 group B9-1: L10.1 to L10.5, lang.09, lang.10 (`B91`)

| id | what | why |
|---|---|---|
| B91-01 | The engine's KV lives in the C pool (rt.04) from L10.1 on: `ModelRunner` creates one `KvPool` (shared as `Arc<Mutex<KvPool>>`) and `forward` writes each new token's K and V into the block table the caller passes, as f16 (format v1). Attention gathers a sequence's K and V into contiguous f32 and calls `tl_flash_attn_fwd_f32` with `q_offset` for prefill, chunks, and decode alike; `tl_paged_attn_decode_f32` (L9.4) is not used. L10.1 therefore lists rt.04 in `deps` (the catalog row lists it only for L10.4). | One attention path keeps chunk invariance bit for bit (FlashAttention tiles align to absolute key positions; mixing it with the paged kernel for decode would not be bitwise equal); a pool from the start means L10.2 to L10.4 only hand out block ids. |
| B91-02 | Runner logits are compared with HF's float32 forward at atol `2e-2` (1e-3 of the largest logit; measured 7e-3), not 1e-4 against your Python, and greedy streams under the near-tie rule with margin `4e-2`. The comparison with your Python runs through MS-L10 (`tokens-equal`) and `ss parity`, not the course tests. | K and V pass through f16 (rounding 2^-11), which your Python's f32 cache (L7.9, L8.2) does not; a Rust test cannot call your Python. |
| B91-03 | `sample()` takes `(logits, params, prompt, output, rng)` and returns `(u32, f64)`, not `(logits: &mut [f32], p, history, rng) -> (u32, f32)`. | spec/sampling.md applies the repetition penalty over prompt and output but presence and frequency over the output only, so the two histories must be separate; the logprob parity with L8.1 is bit-exact in f64. |
| B91-04 | The Rust sampler sorts in Rust; it does not call ds.04's `tl_topk_f32` (ds.04's comment expected it). ds.04 is not a dep of L10.1. | The kept set must follow spec/sampling.md's `(logit desc, id asc)` order over f64 penalized logits; the C top-k works on f32. |
| B91-05 | L10.2 to L10.4 are joined by two traits in sched.rs: `BlockSpace` (allocate, append_slot, release, block_table) with the free-list implementation `FreeListBlocks`, and `PrefillPolicy` with `WholePrompt`; L10.3's `Chunked` and L10.4's `BlockManager` implement them. `Scheduler::new(cfg, blocks)` is generic over the block space; `Chunk` carries `last` (sample after it). The `Engine` that composes runner, scheduler, block manager, and samplers is a new unit `tl-engine/src/engine.rs` owned by L10.5. | DESIGN's `Scheduler::new(cfg, blocks: BlockManager)` would make L10.2 depend on the later L10.4; the seams let each module be finished and tested in order, and the engine needs all four. |
| B91-06 | Aging uses a fixed heap key `priority * aging_steps - arrival` (rank under `Priority`), equivalent to ranking by `priority + age / aging_steps`; the engine sets `aging_steps = 64`. L10.1 adds its binding as two new tl-sys units, `kernels.rs` and `kv.rs`, which L10.0's crate root declares and re-exports (`pub mod kernels; pub mod kv; pub use kernels::*; pub use kv::*;`, four glue lines in L10.0's `lib.rs`); the tl-engine crate root stays L8.4's unit and declares every engine module (Q7). | B111-01 | `M08.4` has `deps = []` and `reading = ["M08.3", "S-M08", "M04.2"]`. Its golden tests write M08.3's cross-entropy gradient, $X^\top(\mathrm{softmax} - \mathrm{onehot})/n$, in numpy instead of calling `cross_entropy_vjp`. (`B111` = authoring group B11-1: M08.4, L11.1 to L11.3, ethics.03, ethics.04.) | `hvp.py` calls no other unit, and a test-only call would make the 3.4 mirror rule demand `M08.4` in M08.3's `used_by` (another group's row) for no call site in the system. |
| B111-02 | `checkpoint_schedule(n, B)` returns segment start layers (`[0, ...]`) of the schedule with the fewest recomputed layers whose peak is at most `B`, under the cost model of `torch.utils.checkpoint.checkpoint_sequential` (every segment but the last recomputed): peak = max_i(i + s_i), recomputed = n - s_last. The optimum uses shrinking segments (least peak = least P with P(P+1)/2 >= n, about sqrt(2n)); ties go to the largest-first fill. `M08.4` adds `hessian_fd`, `checkpoint_cost`, and `min_checkpoint_memory` to the 4.1 signatures. | The catalog's "segment boundaries (sqrt(n))" leaves the rule open; uniform sqrt(n) segments peak at about 2 sqrt(n) - 1 and are not optimal under the model. A brute force over all 2^(n-1) schedules for n <= 10 is a course test. |
| B111-03 | `L11.1`, `L11.3`, and `ethics.04` have `used_by = []`, so verify check 9 ("build module has no call site") fails for them. Their catalog call sites are the C1 trainer (`--bf16 --accum --checkpoint-activations`, `--world 4 --zero 2`) and dur.12's release gate, which reads the safety rows as JSON from a subprocess and so lists no Python module in `deps`. C1 is not registered while this group ran. | The 3.4 mirror rule accepts only registered modules whose `deps` name the module (as B41-04, B53-02). Shared change: C1's row lists `L11.1` and `ethics.04` (and `L11.3` with its `--world` option) in `deps`, and these rows set `used_by = ["C1"]`. |
| B111-04 | `L11.1` emulates mixed precision with a differentiable `cast(x, dtype)` (forward and backward both rounded by M09.1) and `autocast(dtype)` for `bf16` and `fp16`, which wraps `tinyllm.autograd.tensor.Tensor.__matmul__` and `__rmatmul__` process-wide while the outermost block is open (restored in `finally`, nesting by a stack). Additions to the 4.3 signatures: `cast`, `autocast`, `autocast_dtype`; `DynamicLossScaler.step(opt, params, clip=None)` with `loss_scale`, `good_steps`, `last_grad_norm`, `state_dict`, `load_state_dict`; `grad_accumulate(..., scaler=None)` weighting by n_i / N; `train_step_mixed`; `checkpoint(fn, *args, params=(), rngs=())` and `checkpoint_sequential(layers, x, mem_budget_layers, rngs=())`. | Every matmul of every model goes through `Tensor.__matmul__` (F.matmul, Linear, attention), and L0.1's contract has no autocast hook; the wrapper reaches all of them without editing L0.1. fp16 is needed for loss scaling to have anything to do. `params` and `rngs` are what make checkpointed gradients bitwise equal (parameters as parents, dropout replayed). |
| B111-05 | Activation checkpointing's memory test asserts the bytes held after the forward pass are below half of the plain run's and the overall tracemalloc peak below 0.9 of it, not the M08.4 peak ratio. | The L0.1 `backward()` keeps the outer graph (its toposort list) alive until it returns, so the last segment and the checkpoints stay resident while earlier segments are recomputed; PyTorch frees saved tensors as backward consumes them, which is what M08.4's model assumes. The chapter says so. |
| B111-06 | `L11.2` moves messages over `multiprocessing.Pipe` (one full-duplex pipe per pair of ranks), not shared memory; `spawn` uses the "spawn" start method, returns each rank's result, raises `RuntimeError` with a failing rank's traceback, and `TimeoutError` after `timeout`. `Comm` adds `send`, `recv`, `bytes_sent`, and module-level `chunk_bounds` (numpy.array_split sizes); in each ring step even ranks send first and odd ranks receive first. | Pipes give ordered point-to-point messages without a hand-built mailbox protocol over shared memory; the collectives, the byte accounting, and the deadlock rule are the same. "spawn" behaves the same on macOS and Linux and never forks a process holding BLAS threads. |
| B111-07 | `L11.3`'s `DDP` averages gradients when `sync_grads()` is called (one flat bucket, `all_reduce(op="mean")`) instead of through backward hooks; `ZeroOptimizer` keeps a float64 master copy of the rank's chunk of all parameters flattened in `parameters()` order and wraps `opt_cls([chunk])`, adds `gather()` (stage 3 rebuilds full parameters before forward) and `memory_bytes()`; stage 3 releases parameters to zero-size arrays between steps. The per-rank memory test computes M05.1's per-parameter accounting (params + grads + 2 AdamW moments) itself instead of calling `memory_plan`. | L0.1 has no backward hooks. `memory_plan` takes a Llama `ModelConfig`; the test model is a 44-parameter MLP whose shares divide exactly by 4 ranks. |
| B111-08 | `ethics.04` scores toxicity with a hand-written lexicon (noisy-OR over whole-word phrases) and refusals with a phrase list, rates with Wilson intervals, scorer quality with M07.7's ROC-AUC and a step-sum average precision (not a trapezoid PR-AUC). Report rows use `metric = "score"` with `task` naming the rate (`toxicity-rate`, `refusal-rate`, `over-refusal-rate`, `toxicity-scorer`, `bias-gap:<axis>`, `stereotype-preference`); `gate(report, limits)` compares interval bounds. `M01.4` is `reading`: its trapezoid is inside M07.7's `roc_auc`. | The catalog fixes "refusal and toxicity rates on fixture sets, ROC-AUC" but no scorer; a classifier needs a model and a network. `formats/eval-results.schema.json` has no `rate` or `auc` metric (shared change: add them); trapezoids in precision-recall space overstate the area. |
| B111-09 | `ethics.03` lists `contract = []` (the card links `templates/MODEL_CARD.md`, `templates/DATASHEET.md`, and `formats/checkpoint.md`); its artifacts are `docs/MODEL_CARD.md`, `docs/DATASHEET.md`, and `docs/models/smollm2-135m-instruct.md`. C1 cannot be trained in an authoring run (hours), and a reference card with invented capstone numbers would teach the opposite of the module. |
